# Requirement Traceability Matrix

This matrix maps every requirement ID from [00 §1](00-understanding-and-plan.md#1-scope-confirmation--requirement-checklist) to the design sections that specify it, the code that implements it, the tests that prove it and its verified status. Status legend: **Implemented and tested** means the requirement is implemented and automated tests assert its core behaviour (any untested sub-part is named in Notes); **Implemented (verified by build / manual run only)** means the code exists, is compiled on CI (for app and plug-in code, see the CI note below) and is exercised headlessly or by hand, but no automated test asserts the requirement's behaviour; **Partially implemented** means only part of the requirement exists or is verified, and Notes say which part. Tests are `TEST_CASE` names from `tests/*.cpp`, all compiled into the `flub_tests` binary, which ctest runs in every CI job that runs tests (`core` on GCC, Clang, MSVC and Apple Clang; `sanitizers` with ASan + UBSan; `rtsan` with the Clang 20 RealtimeSanitizer). `tests/CMakeLists.txt` also compiles the `tools/flubsound-cli` sources except `main.cpp` into `flub_tests`, so the CLI cases in `tests/test_offline_render.cpp` run in every one of those jobs, independent of `FLUB_BUILD_TOOLS`. The exceptions are the RTSan self-test (compiled only with `FLUB_RTSAN`), `tests/test_platform_linux.cpp` (Linux builds only) and the C half of the driver-ABI layout test (skipped on MSVC). The `App:` cases are not in `flub_tests`: they live in `tests/app/*.cpp` and form a second binary, `flub_app_tests`, which compiles the desktop app's own sources (all but `Main.cpp`) with a fake audio device, a fake per-app router and windowless views. It is built only with the app (`FLUB_BUILD_APP=ON`, `FLUB_BUILD_APP_TESTS=ON`), and the CI `app + plugin` job runs it with ctest on windows-2022, macos-14 and ubuntu-24.04. Design links point into `docs/`, `platform/`, `presets/README.md` and the top-level `README.md`; Code paths are relative to the repository root. Figures in Notes say whether a test asserts them, a design doc records them as an author measurement, or a manual or scratch-harness run produced them.

**Verification baseline and CI state.** The rows were re-verified against the **current tree**: the files this matrix ships with, including the matched-bypass reference limiter, the PDP device profile, UTF-8 file paths, the Gaming upward-only compressor rule, the AutoDrive floor at the requested drive and the mode overrides in the published effective values. Test counts and local results refer to that tree, not to a commit. **Phase 3 batch 5 (docs/11 §5.4; local, not yet on CI).** CI still cannot start a job on this private repository (Actions billing), so batches 3, 4 and 5 have not run on CI. ## R1 Real-time audio engine

Interpretations used (from [00 §1](00-understanding-and-plan.md#r1-real-time-audio-engine)):

- **R1.1** is the latency Flubsound **adds** end to end on top of the OS and the application. One budget, with three scope labels (01 §5): **chain** ≈2.1 ms (Low Latency, 100 samples) or 4.0 ms (Balanced, 192) at 48 kHz; **app engine** 3.0 ms (144) or 5.4 ms (260) with the master limiter; **added end-to-end** (estimate, Windows driver path) ≈9.5 ms in Low Latency, the profile that meets ≤ 10 ms, and ≈12–13 ms in Balanced, the upper edge. Today's capture paths add their own buffering (01 §5.3). Quality (≈28 ms chain) is for music and batch use.
- **R1.2** is the JUCE 9 device layer. PipeWire and PulseAudio are reached through the ALSA / JACK compatibility layers for I/O and through `pactl` for routing; a native PipeWire node is roadmap.
- **R1.3**: all DSP is float; metering integrators and long-running accumulators use double where precision matters.
- **R1.4**: every coefficient is derived from the session rate; the chain test covers 11 rates from 8 to 192 kHz.
- **R1.5**: lock-free SPSC FIFOs, no allocation or locks on the audio thread (enforced by tests), drift-compensated resampling between clock domains (`DriftCompensatedFifo` for per-app captures), and denormal protection.

| ID | Design | Code | Tests | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R1.1**<br>Latency under 10–12 ms | [01 §5 Latency budget](01-architecture.md#5-latency-budget)<br>[01 §5.1 Algorithmic latency per chain at 48 kHz: 100 smp = 2.08 ms (Low Latency), 192 = 4.00 ms (Balanced), 1352 = 28.17 ms (Quality). App engine including the MixEngine master limiter: 144 smp = 3.0 ms, 260 = 5.4 ms, 1420 = 29.6 ms. The MixEngine look-ahead test asserts these exact counts (192+48+20, 100+24+20, 1352+48+20) and checks that an impulse comes out exactly at the reported latency for Balanced, for Low Latency, and for a Low Latency strip padded to a Balanced one (the padding proof); for Quality it checks only the count. The chain test asserts Balanced < 5 ms and Low Latency < 2.5 ms. Constant latency under bypass is proved by the ModuleSlot test (error ≤ 1e-5) and the all-bypassed chain (a pure delay, error ≤ 1e-6), not by the chain-latency test.<br><br>Re-measured with `getLatencySamples()` (strip / app engine, for Low Latency, Balanced, Quality):<br>• 44.1 kHz: 96/138, 182/246, 1332/1396 smp<br>• 48 kHz: 100/144, 192/260, 1352/1420 smp<br>• 96 kHz: 148/216, 312/428, 1592/1708 smp<br>• 192 kHz: 244/360, 552/764, 2072/2284 smp<br>The strip figures match 03 §15.1 and 01 §5.1.<br><br>**Not verified: the 10–12 ms end-to-end target itself.** The ≈9.5 ms (Low Latency + WASAPI exclusive) and ≈12–13 ms (Balanced + IAudioClient3 shared) in 01 §5.2 are estimates for the Windows virtual-driver path, and that driver is design only (not built). There is no loopback measurement (roadmap item 1.2; device-lab test 8 in 10 §5). The UI figure is reported, not measured: device in/out as the driver states them, plus engine, plus the capture FIFO target. WASAPI exclusive and IAudioClient3 cannot be exercised in CI.<br><br>Today's Windows per-app process-loopback path adds the `DriftCompensatedFifo` target, max(2 blocks, packet + block) + 4 frames (asserted in the DriftFifo test). With typical 10 ms (480-frame) packets and 128-frame blocks that is 612 frames = 12.75 ms, so this path alone exceeds the target before any device buffering. On Linux the PipeWire quantum dominates (1024/48000 = 21 ms by default on many distros) unless it is forced to 256 by hand; per-stream `node.latency` is roadmap. 01 §5.3 now states both.<br><br>**One budget.** 01 §5 is the single latency model, with explicit scopes (chain / app engine / added end-to-end). 00 R1.1, the 07 exit criteria, 10 §5 test 8, the README and the Windows driver README §6 use its figures: the driver README now counts the Low Latency engine as 100 + 24 + 20 = 144 samples (3.0 ms) and states the R1.1 goal (≤ 10 ms added in Low Latency) instead of its former ≤ 15 ms.<br><br>Wave B2 (docs/11 E40 part 3 / E42a; local runs only, green on CI in run 17): a strip is padded only within its sync group, and by default every strip is its own group, so Game on Low Latency beside Music on Quality stays at 100 + 68 samples (3.5 ms) instead of 1352 + 68 (29.6 ms), impulse-verified per strip. The gate frame is 21.3 ms at every rate (Quality 26.8–30.2 ms from 44.1 to 192 kHz), and below 32 kHz Quality runs as Balanced (8 kHz: 1152 → 92 samples, 144 → 11.5 ms). The app's readout is per strip from the MixEngine's own figures (default layout at 48 kHz: Game 168, Music 1420 samples; Game and Music in one group: Game 168 + 1252 padding), is labelled an estimate ("~" in the header, "(estimated)" in its tooltip) and reads "--" while no device runs or the loopback guard holds the output. Still no measurement: E42d.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 2 batch 2 (green in CI run 36391842718): E48a's Low Latency request is used: on Linux, Low Latency chosen by hand exports `PIPEWIRE_LATENCY=128/48000` with `node.lock-quantum` and re-opens a JACK / ALSA device (a user's own values win; `App UI: the PipeWire quantum request follows the latency profile ...`), not run on a real PipeWire system. E05 step 3 kept Quality's 2 ms look-ahead (3–5 ms deepened the ducking under kicks), and E10's rate-aware saturator designs keep the 32 / 16-sample latencies, so no chain latency changed. E42a: a preset made for another profile no longer changes the profile silently; the app offers the switch.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 3 batch 1 (green in CI run 36521915726): E42d's loopback probe exists: `flub::latency::LatencyProbe` (exponential sweeps, regularised deconvolution, a 30 dB SNR gate, the median of the runs, a reference channel for recordings not locked to playback) and `flubsound-cli latency-probe generate | analyze`. Offline it finds integer delays to 0.000 samples at 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz, a fractional, attenuated, inverted and noisy delay within 0.06 samples, and the chain's own latency as 1351.997 / 191.997 / 99.997 samples against the reported 1352 / 192 / 100 (`LatencyProbe (E42d): ...` 6 cases, `CLI: latency-probe generate / analyze on files (E42d)`). It has not been run on a real interface, so the end-to-end figures are still estimates. |

`AudioBuffer` owns float storage<br>`core/include/flub/dsp/Processor.h`: every module's `process(const AudioBlock&)`<br>Double where precision matters: `core/include/flub/dsp/Svf.h` (design in double), `core/include/flub/dsp/FirDesign.h`, `core/include/flub/dsp/Biquad.h` (double coefficients and state), `core/include/flub/dsp/EnvelopeFollower.h` (`MeanSquareFollower`), `core/include/flub/dsp/Compressor.h` (double gain and time coefficients), `core/include/flub/dsp/TruePeakLimiter.h` (double box sum and gain envelope), `core/src/analysis/LoudnessMeter.cpp`, `core/include/flub/engine/Protection.h` (gated loudness sums)<br>Hosts: `app/Source/engine/AudioEngineHost.cpp` (JUCE float callback), `plugin/Source/PluginProcessor.h` (float `processBlock` only; the double-precision `processBlock` is not supported)<br>`core/src/io/WavFile.cpp`, `tools/flubsound-cli/CliOptions.cpp`, `tools/flubsound-cli/OfflineRenderer.cpp`: PCM16/24/32 and float64 decode to float; export f32 by default, or pcm24 / pcm16 with TPDF dither | Type-level: every audio-processing test drives float32 planar buffers through the modules, chain and mixer.<br>Precision evidence:<br>• `tests/test_engine.cpp` `Chain: everything bypassed = input delayed by the chain latency (bit-transparent path)`<br>• `tests/test_limiter.cpp` `TruePeakLimiter: a -30 dBFS signal passes bit-exactly, only delayed by latencySamples()` (44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz)<br>• `tests/test_compressor.cpp` `Compressor [adversarial]: the gain lands on the static curve even with the slowest times at 192 kHz`<br>• `tests/test_primitives.cpp` `OnePoleSmoother: glides converge exactly to non-zero targets (no float stall)`<br>• `tests/test_loudness_meter.cpp` `LoudnessMeter: EBU Tech 3341 case 1 (-23 dBFS, 20 s) at 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz`, `LoudnessMeter: 192 kHz still meets Tech 3341 case 1`<br>• `tests/test_wav.cpp` `WavFile: Float32 multichannel round trip is bit exact and header fields read back`, `WavFile review: PCM round trips stay within spec at full scale and at every sample rate` | Implemented and tested | Float32 is enforced by the type system: `AudioBlock` carries only `float*`, every `Processor::process` takes an `AudioBlock`, and the plug-in has no double-precision path. No test asserts the format as such. The evidence is that every audio-processing case runs float32 buffers (not all 560 cases: the JSON, preset, device-profile, driver-ABI, platform and CLI folder-walk cases process no audio), plus these precision results:<br>• the all-bypassed chain is a pure delay within 1e-6;<br>• the limiter passes -30 dBFS bit-exactly at 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz;<br>• EBU Tech 3341 case 1 reads -23 LUFS within ±0.1 LU (momentary, short-term, integrated) at 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz with 20 s of tone, and at 192 kHz with 5 s, using double integrators;<br>• the compressor gain lands within 0.01 dB of the static curve at 192 kHz with the slowest times; a float one-pole had stalled 0.74 dB short (03 §9), which is why that state is double;<br>• the float32 WAV round trip is bit exact.<br><br>Converting 16/24-bit device formats (USB headsets) to float is done by the OS / JUCE and is not tested here.<br><br>03 §0.1 lists every double-precision place: SVF / FIR design, Biquad, MeanSquareFollower, the loudness-gate sums and LoudnessMeter energies, the TruePeakLimiter gain envelope and box-filter sum, the Compressor's gain smoother and coefficients, and the StereoSpatializer correlation. |

module). MixEngine, the app's multi-strip engine, is tested only at 48 kHz; its master limiter is unit-tested at every rate. The host's device-rate handling is build-only.<br><br>The chain test runs 11 rates (8, 16, 22.05, 24, 32, 44.1, 48, 88.2, 96, 176.4, 192 kHz) in Music and Gaming mode, with Boost and all five macros at 100 % and the compressor and saturator on. It asserts that the output is finite on both channels, that both channels are not silent and peak at ≤ -1 dBFS, and that chain latency is < 6 ms at ≥ 22.05 kHz (< 12 ms at 8 / 16 kHz).<br><br>Strip latency re-measured (Low Latency / Balanced / Quality):<br>• 44.1 kHz: 96 / 182 / 1332 smp<br>• 96 kHz: 148 / 312 / 1592 smp (1.54 / 3.25 / 16.58 ms)<br>• 192 kHz: 244 / 552 / 2072 smp (1.27 / 2.88 / 10.79 ms)<br><br>True peak:<br>• the limiter asserts ≤ +0.1 dB true peak on band-limited programme at 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz, and at 192 kHz with the profile look-aheads, with the safety clamp never engaged;<br>• 03 §10.6 measured worst cases: band-limited noise -0.023 to -0.031 dB re ceiling; raw full-band white noise up to +1.35 dB (44.1 kHz, 0.5 ms look-ahead);<br>• the unit tests bound raw full-band synthetic signals loosely (≤ +2 dB, `full-band synthetic signals hold the sample ceiling exactly`) and CD-band noise flat to 0.45 fs at ≤ +0.15 dB and ≥ −0.5 dB re ceiling (a separate adversarial test at 44.1 / 48 kHz; its six cases measure −0.05 to −0.03 dB).<br><br>Loudness: Tech 3341 case 1 is within ±0.1 LU at 44.1 / 48 / 96 / 192 kHz.<br><br>Limitations:<br>• the app just runs at the rate the output device opens with; there is no mismatch warning (08 A6, roadmap);<br>• a device rate change restarts the device; the engine is rebuilt in `audioDeviceAboutToStart` (not via the 5 Hz timer) and its output fades in over 10 ms, so the restart is a short silence without a step (the fade-in is asserted in `App: engine swap across a device restart (pending, mid-crossfade, while stopped) is safe and fades in`, green on CI in run 16);<br>• HRIR sets are never resampled; the parametric renderer is used instead;<br>• the air exciter is disabled below 42 kHz;<br>• the CLI has no offline SRC (r8brain is planned);<br>• `DriftCompensatedFifo`'s Hermite interpolator does not band-limit, so captures are requested at the device rate;<br>• rate changes on real hardware (A2DP ↔ HFP, 44.1 / 96 kHz devices) are covered only by the device-lab plan (10 §5 tests 1 and 6), not by CI;<br>• the virtual driver's float32 / 48 kHz default format is design only. |

Allocation counts asserted:<br>• 0 in `ProcessingChain::process` in all three profiles, including a mid-stream mode switch, A/B bank switch and global bypass;<br>• 0 in `MixEngine::process`, including 3 s at Low Latency with the master limiting by at least 2 dB;<br>• 0 in `ParameterStore::snapshot`;<br>• 0 in every module's process / reset / setters;<br>• 0 allocations, 0 frees and (Linux / glibc) 0 mutex locks per block in the app's device callback (`flub_app_tests`, see below).<br><br>RTSan: `ProcessingChain::process`, `MixEngine::process`, the 12 `Processor::process` overrides (the 11 modules and `AsyncModelProcessor`), every `Processor::reset` override and `TransientShaper::reset`, and the setters the audio thread calls (every module's `setParams`, the two EQs' `setBand`, `ParametricEq::setOutputGainDb`, the `TransientShaper` setters) are `[[clang::nonblocking]]`; `test_rtsan.cpp` static_asserts them and proves a violation aborts. The `reset()` and setter annotations are green in the CI `rtsan` job (run 12). TransientShaper, ModuleSlot and Protection `process()` are not annotated themselves, but RTSan checks them at run time because they are called inside the annotated chain. The CI `rtsan` job passed in all four completed runs, most recently 36248480584.<br><br>`DriftCompensatedFifo`, in an event-driven simulation of 90–120 s at ±200 and ±2000 ppm, with 480- and 441-frame packets and 128 / 512-frame blocks:<br>• overflows and dropped frames are 0 over the whole run;<br>• no underrun occurs after settling (and none at all for positive drift);<br>• the fill stays within ±1 ms of its target, max(2 blocks, packet + block) + 4 frames;<br>• the learned correction is within 5 ppm of the true drift;<br>• no sample step exceeds the clean 997 Hz sine's;<br>• `push()` / `pull()` make 0 allocations;<br>• a 200 ms capture stall is exactly one counted underrun, with a click-free fade-out and 5 ms fade-in, and the learned drift survives (±10 ppm);<br>• a 300 ms device stall is one counted overflow of ≈0.3 s of audio, after which the fill returns to target.<br><br>Overload and FIFO telemetry in the app (`tests/app/test_app_overload.cpp`, green on CI in run 12, all three OSes):<br>• the watchdog enters after 4 consecutive polls at ≥ 90 % load, or 3 new glitches within 10 polls, and clears after 10 calm polls (below 75 %, no new glitch); 200 polls hovering around the entry threshold (never 4 hot in a row) start nothing, and once overloaded 400 polls between the two thresholds or with calm stretches shorter than 10 polls end nothing; a restarted counter is re-based, not read as a jump; a stopped device clears the state;<br>• a start and an end each broadcast `Change::Device`; the header readout turns into OVERLOAD with the recommended action in its tooltip, and Settings › Processing counts the session's episodes;<br>• a fake `juce::AudioIODeviceType` opened through `AudioEngineHost::openDevice` reports 3 xruns, which reach the header readout, the tooltip, the Settings line and the watchdog (a 3-glitch burst starts an overload at low load);<br>• a fake capture driven through the device callback with a 40-block gap and a 300 ms device stall produces at least one underrun and one overflow with dropped frames, and `EngineController::getCaptureStreams()` hands the host's `Stats` unchanged to the Settings / tooltip text.<br><br>Opt-in automatic overload response (`tests/app/test_app_overload_response.cpp`, 6 cases, green on CI in run 14; Settings › Processing, default off):<br>• `AutoLoadReducer` fed polls directly steps one level after exactly 6 overloaded polls with the switch on, Quality → Balanced → Low Latency, and does nothing more at the bottom; after a step the next needs 60 polls, however long the overload lasts; it never steps back up; with the switch off nothing counts, and an overload counts only from when it is switched on; a manual profile change (or a profile that does not match its last step) resets the ladder and starts the 60-poll hold-off;<br>• through `EngineController` with chosen statuses: the step is written to every strip and both banks and re-prepared into the chains, `Change::Device` is broadcast, the header tooltip, the tray text and Settings › Processing describe it and Restore returns to the user's profile; with the switch off an overload changes nothing.<br>The decision runs on the message thread (2 Hz timer), never on the audio thread; each step is a latency-profile change, which now goes through the crossfaded engine swap (a 2 × 10 ms dip, above); it stays opt-in and rate-limited.<br><br>Denormals: the host holds FTZ/DAZ, and each module flushes its own state. The saturator and virtualiser decay to exact zero even with FTZ off (x86).<br><br>**Why Partially implemented:** the requirement's outcome (glitch-free on the real audio thread) is not exercised.<br>• The app's own callback code (`AudioEngineHost::audioDeviceIOCallbackWithContext` → `processBlock`, capture-slot hand-off) is not RTSan-annotated. `flub_app_tests` starts the host with a fake `juce::AudioIODevice` (48 kHz / 256) as JUCE does and drives 600 callbacks from a separate thread: 8 device inputs into the 7.1 strip, a fake per-app capture through `DriftCompensatedFifo` with a 40-block gap (underrun, re-prime), strip gain / mute, master ceiling and parameter changes, and the analyser taps drained between blocks. Every block has 0 allocations, 0 frees and, on Linux / glibc only, 0 `pthread_mutex_lock` / `_trylock` calls (a mutation check confirmed that an added allocation or `juce::CriticalSection` in the callback fails it). The first 8 blocks are not probed, because the first callback on a new device thread promotes it, which may allocate once. Other blocking calls (futex waits, system calls, spin locks) are not detected. Green on CI (run 11, all three OSes).<br>• The Windows `ProcessLoopbackCapture` thread and real device callbacks do not run in CI; `DriftCompensatedFifo` is proven only in simulation.<br>• Structural changes while the device runs (latency profile, strip layout, a neural model install or removal) now go through the crossfaded engine swap (round 8, green on CI in run 16): the new engine is built on the message thread while the old one plays, pre-rolls its latency + 10 ms, then takes over in a 10 ms equal-gain crossfade (same latency) or a 2 × 10 ms dip (latency change, where the programme jumps by the latency difference); the old engine is freed on the message thread. Asserted with a fake device: no sample step beyond the fade's own slope bound, no gap longer than the fade, 100 swaps with a superseded request and no allocation, free or lock on the audio thread, a device restart before, during and after a swap, and a removed strip's tail played out. The new engine starts with fresh detector state (AutoLevel, limiters), so a slow loop's level can differ from the old engine's after the swap; a device format change restarts the device (a 10 ms fade-in, no crossfade).<br>• The CPU-overload watchdog notifies by default; the opt-in automatic response (one latency-profile step down at a time) is tested with chosen load / glitch samples and a fake device's xrun count, never against a real overload.<br>• There is no real-device soak or glitch detector (07 items 1.1 and 1.10; an 8 h zero-dropout soak is a Phase 1 exit criterion).<br>• The SystemTuning test only proves Linux promote/revert restores the policy; promotion itself needs RT rights, and RealtimeKit is roadmap.<br>• Driver clock slaving is design only.<br><br>08 D1 describes this simulation coverage and what it leaves out.<br><br>Wave B2 (local runs only, green on CI in run 17): the docs/11 E51 Phase A loopback guard is tested with a fake device in the exact real-world case (preferred output removed, input "CABLE Output", default "CABLE Input"): output peak 0 over the 200 looped blocks, banner kind LoopbackPair, governor scale 1 → 1 after the headset returns; the guard's audio-thread path allocates, frees and locks nothing. A device rate change now restarts running captures at the new rate instead of resampling 3:1 without band-limiting (hardening #34), and a slot released while the callback is stalled is quarantined instead of re-allocated under the reader (#38). One xrun burst at low load no longer steps the automatic overload response (#13). Open: the full banner with Retry / Choose output / Open sound settings, explicit device selection, real devices.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 2 batch 2 (green in CI run 36391842718): E45: every device callback's duration and interval go into lock-free histograms (`flub::CallbackTiming`, 1/8-octave buckets, p99.9 and max; `tests/test_callback_timing.cpp`, `App: the device callback's duration and interval histograms ...`), nothing reads them in the UI yet. E27 step (4): a JUCE ALSA device's 5.1 / 7.1 order is permuted into the engine's (the centre had landed in the rear left), tested with a fake 8-input ALSA device. The capture FIFO's resampler is a 48-tap polyphase Kaiser sinc (E50 Phase A). A rotating diagnostics log and crash reports (E54) count glitches at 2 Hz from the existing atomics.<br><br>Phase 3 batch 3 (local only, not yet on CI): a strip fed silence for 10 s plus its latency is frozen (its chain not run) and wakes with a 10 ms pre-roll and a 5 ms fade; frozen-then-resumed strips null against continuous processing and a fed-silence strip's callback time drops by at least 90 %; the overload watchdog judges the p99.9 callback of each poll's window and counts late callbacks for a device without its own xrun count; the capture FIFO's surround downmix folds the LFE as the chain does.<br><br>Phase 3 batch 4 (local only, not yet on CI): a Flubsound FX state that is loaded and saved again is the same bytes, and every parameter, a new instance and all 30 factory presets round-trip bit for bit in values, state and audio (0 of 4 cases passed before the fix); pluginval at strictness 10 passes on Linux here (Windows and macOS wait for CI); the nightly soak runs the five user rows (clicks still found, not triaged: 10 in 2 minutes per row, 13 on the base commit, so not new); CI archives the build's symbols. The update check is off by default and never downloads or installs; it has not run against GitHub (no release yet). |

AutoDrive (loudness target, reduce-only) is tested through the chain (`Chain: AutoDrive pulls a hot drive down to the loudness target at <= 2 dB/s and settles near, not above, it`, `Chain: AutoDrive never raises the drive: below the target it is inert, and it stops at 0 dB drive (the input itself)`, `Chain: AutoDrive's reduction stops at the requested drive, so it recovers at once`; details in R3.2). The former LoudnessMatch class (replaced by `ComparisonMatcher` in docs/11 E37, R2.10) had unit tests of its own (converges to the measured wet − dry difference at exactly 3 dB/s without overshoot; ±12 dB bound, hold while either side is silent, `reset()`), besides the chain-level assertions (R2.10, R3.2). "Multi-band" is implemented as per-band dynamic EQ, not a crossover multiband compressor; the only crossover-based stage is the maximizer glue (R2.1).<br><br>The ≥ 10-band requirement is asserted only as a constant (`kEqBands >= 10`); no chain-level test drives the `eq.N.*` parameters through ProcessingChain (factory-preset renders exercise them without asserting EQ response).<br><br>Limitations: no linear-phase mode (roadmap, 03 §2.9); type / slope / enable changes fade the band's wet mix out and back in over about 5 ms each way (an audible "flash", not a click); the dynamic EQ has no look-ahead. The EQ curve editor and card UI are compiled only in the CI `app` job, with no automated UI test.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 2 batch 1 (green in CI run 36367474107): Gaming mode bands 4 / 5 are the background-relative cue enhancer (`DynEqMode::CueLift`, docs/11 E19). New output-device correction (`DeviceCorrection`, docs/11 E15 / E11): up to 16 RBJ sections per channel from an AutoEQ / Equalizer APO ParametricEQ.txt, on the master sum before the limiter, with an automatic preamp from the static max-boost predictor; response within 0.0003 dB of an independent biquad reference, no sine grows. Real AutoEQ files are not tested (licence review), and the Windows / macOS app paths are compiled, not run. |

below 0 dBFS it is the identity (docs/11 E28a)`<br>• `Focus ILD method: HRTF-rendered sources at 15 degree steps through the focus off / 50 / 100 %`<br>• `Focus ILD method: a synthetic known ILD reads back per band, and the HRTF-rendered sources carry the model's own ILD`<br>• `HeadphoneVirtualizer: Classic is the default and ignores frontBack bit for bit; switching the renderer glides without a click and lands on the target design (docs/11 E28)`<br>• `HeadphoneVirtualizer: Enhanced renderer - 4-8 kHz peak-to-notch of a correlated 7-speaker impulse and the diffuse field (docs/11 E28, pinned)`<br>• `HeadphoneVirtualizer: Enhanced renderer - FL vs BL >= 3 dB in all four directional bands, FC vs sides tilt within 1 dB, the sphere's ITD and ILD kept (docs/11 E28)`<br>• `HeadphoneVirtualizer: Enhanced renderer - bit-exact under random block partitions with renderer switches, and the chain hands virt.renderer / virt.frontBack over (docs/11 E28)`<br>• `HeadphoneVirtualizer: Enhanced renderer - the level match keeps virt on vs off within 0.5 LU for 5.1 / 7.1 pink, correlated or not (docs/11 E28)`<br>• `Spatial metrics: IACC, ITD, DRR and 1/3-octave levels read their known values on synthetic binaural impulses`<br>• `Spatial metrics: a synthetic pinna notch moves the diffuse-field deviation at its band, and a shift of the notch follows it`<br>• `Spatial metrics: parametricHrir is the virtualiser's own rendering of a speaker, sample for sample`<br>• `Spatial metrics: splitImpulses separates responses at least 100 ms apart and keeps each one's reflections`<br>• `Spatial metrics: today's parametric renderer reproduces its weak values (7.1, defaults)` | Implemented and tested | Mono compatibility holds by construction because every stage acts on S only. Measured worst \|L'+R'-(L+R)\| = 1.19e-7 over 40 randomised full-scale runs (03 §7.3.6); the test bound is 1e-5.<br><br>Asserted by tests:<br>• width 2 gives +6.02 ± 0.1 dB of S above the low cut;<br>• auto mono safety: the effective width settles to 1.0 (± 1e-3) on antiphase noise with per-step jumps ≤ 0.05 (the doc measurement is w_eff ≤ 1.05 within 0.31 s);<br>• virtualiser toggle on 7.1: largest step within 1.25× steady state;<br>• gaming imaging: positional focus is an ILD emphasis around 3 kHz (+6.0 ± 0.05 dB of S at focus 1 on a panned source, M = 4 S); its polarity guard keeps a hard-left sine or white noise exactly silent in the right ear with the left ear bit-identical, gives a pure side signal no lift (≤ 0.01 dB) and still raises an R = L/2 source to ≥ 12 dB ILD (analytic 14 dB).<br><br>Zero latency; CPU 26–32 ns (spatializer, including the polarity guard), 78–80 ns (parametric 7.1 virtualiser).<br><br>The Gaming crossfeed = 0 rule and the binaural lock (width 1, space 0, crossfeed 0 after virtualisation) live in ProcessingChain, which writes them into the published effective values. `tests/test_modes.cpp` now asserts both through the full chain: *Gaming: crossfeed is forced off - a hard-left source never leaks into the right ear, whatever the store says* and *Gaming: binaural lock on a 7.1 strip - width 1, space 0 and focus 0 whatever the store asks* (the preset lint also forbids gaming presets from storing crossfeed). An earlier scratch harness gave the same result: crossfeed 1 leaves a 100 Hz side signal at +0.01 dB in Gaming against -7.52 dB in Music.<br><br>Limitations: width above 1 acts on S alone, so it still puts an anti-phase copy of a hard-panned source in the far ear (at the default minimum correlation 0 the mono safety pulls such width back to 1). Positional focus no longer does: its polarity guard bounds the lift by the band's M, so under Positional 100 % a hard-left source's far ear stays at numerical silence (before the guard its ILD fell from infinite to about 10 dB at 3 kHz; the Positional test now asserts ≥ 60 dB). The guard works on the band mix, not per source, so a hard-panned sound under a louder centred one can still be lifted (03 §7.9). The measured-HRIR renderer exists (direct form, capped at 1024 taps) but nothing in app / plugin / tools calls `setHrirSet`; the SOFA loader and partitioned convolution are roadmap (07 item 2.6). The parametric model is a spherical head with no elevation or pinna notches. Virtualiser on/off is a 20 ms level / timing glide.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 0–1 (green on CI in run 16), each measured before and after: stereo content in an 8-channel container no longer loses 16.8 dB in the centre (0.00 dB after the switch to the passthrough fold, click-free; E27); the 7.1 LFE, silent with the virtualiser off and −0.03 dB with it on, now folds at +6.00 / +5.97 dB re one main (E01); Positional Focus at 100 % adds 2.9 dB of ILD at 3 kHz instead of 7.9 dB and none under the binaural lock (E24).<br><br>docs/11 Phase 2 batch 1 (green in CI run 36367474107): E12 Phase A - energy-preserving Bs2b / Meier crossfeed (ITD 0.25–0.27 ms), Space with a presence dip, and a polarity guard on Width (no anti-phase far ear at width 2). The crossfeed presets moved (+0.6 to +1.1 LU on music); golden files re-recorded for them.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 2 batch 2 (green in CI run 36391842718): E28a: the virtualiser matches the BS.775 downmix's loudness (virt on − off, −30 dBFS pink on every speaker: 5.1 / 7.1 +0.82 … +3.83 → −0.16 … 0.00 LU) and holds its fold at 0 dBFS (full-scale correlated 7.1 +11.79 → 0.00 dBFS before the limiter); the render baseline's `surround-7.1` programme moved for 24 presets and was re-recorded. E01: a 5.1 / 7.1 capture read by a stereo strip is folded in the host like the chain folds (the LFE at `virt.lfe`), instead of keeping FL / FR; E27 step (4): the ALSA channel order (R1.5).<br><br>Phase 3 batch 3 (local only): `spatial.crossfeedType` (Bs2b by default, Meier, Mono-safe) with a crossfade on change and bit-identical renders at the default; the BS.775 fold holds full-scale correlated surround at 0 dBFS before the limiter (+6.88 → 0.00 dBFS on 7.1), and the make-up and fold-headroom gains are on `MeterBus` and in `render.stats.fold`.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 3 batch 5 (local, not yet on CI): the Enhanced renderer (opt-in; Classic is the default and bit-identical): FL vs BL near-ear difference >= 3 dB in 4 directional bands (Classic 1), FC vs sides tilt -2.78 -> -0.57 dB, virt on / off within 0.16 LU, 0.65 % of a core; the 4 - 8 kHz peak-to-notch row is not met (28.06 -> 18.88 dB) and Enhanced as the default is gated on the forced-choice test. The C++ spatial metrics (IACC, ITD, DRR, diffuse field) reproduce E28's weak values; the positional focus adds no ILD (<= 0.09 dB) to HRTF-rendered sources, so its redesign-or-remove decision waits for the pointing task. |

Latency):<br>• compressor 3 / 1 / 0.5 ms = 144 / 48 / 24 samples;<br>• limiter 2 / 1.5 / 0.5 ms + 20-sample true-peak detector = 116 / 92 / 44 samples;<br>• master limiter 1 ms (68 samples), or 0.5 ms (44 samples) when every strip runs Low Latency. The MixEngine test asserts exact totals 192+48+20, 100+24+20 and 1352+48+20 (one Quality strip), and that an impulse arrives at exactly that latency.<br><br>True-peak bounds asserted by tests: ≤ +0.1 dB on band-limited programme, ≤ +0.15 dB on full-level CD-band noise, master ≤ ceiling + 0.15 dB with zero safety clamps; sample peak exact. Doc measurement: limiter true peak for content up to 0.45 fs at or below -0.023 dB re ceiling (03 §10.6); a scratch re-run of the CD-band test programme read -0.029 dB worst.<br><br>The compressor's look-ahead is partial anticipation (about 9.5 % of a step covered with a 10 ms attack and 1 ms look-ahead), not a ceiling guarantee; the limiter provides that. CPU 22–23 ns (compressor), 150–168 ns (limiter). All detection is linked across channels (asserted for compressor and limiter).<br><br>Limitations: the compressor peak hold is not user-adjustable (release starts after 12.5–50 ms depending on the sidechain HP), and the limiter release shape is fixed. |

At the 40 dB setting the implementer measured -31.8 dB on white noise with N 1024, limited by musical noise (03 §12.9).<br><br>Latency is fftSize: 1024 samples = 21.3 ms at 48 kHz. For that reason the chain includes the gate **only in the Quality profile**; it is absent in Balanced and Low Latency, so it is unavailable in the default profile and in the gaming profiles. `gate.on` defaults to off and no macro in either MacroMap table touches it. The chain-level gate is only exercised (allocation-free run, Quality latency total); no chain test asserts noise reduction. CPU 262–279 ns (N 1024).<br><br>Limitations: minimum tracking learns any steady sound as noise (`freezeFloor` is the counter-tool); the profile is not persistent across reset or re-activation; output peaks can exceed input peaks (worst 1.49× FS, bounded by the downstream limiter). Neural voice-chat denoise (N1) is designed only, as a roadmap item in docs/09. The gate UX (learn / freeze) is roadmap (07 item 2.8). |

Drive, bass, harmonics and saturation contributions are governed by the SafetyGovernor.<br><br>Asserted by tests:<br>• Boost at 100 %: drive = 8.0 dB, bass = 5.0 dB; drive 4.0 dB at governor scale 0.5; presence ungoverned; at 20 % presence > 0 and drive 0;<br>• Boost and all macros at 100 % on a hot programme: true peak ≤ -1 dBTP + 0.15 dB, sample peak ≤ -1 dBFS, 0 safety clamps.<br><br>CPU for Music with Boost and all macros at 100 %: 1213–1243 ns per stereo sample in Balanced (5.8–6.0 % of a core).<br><br>Per-macro coverage: Punch (engages Clarity, audition test), Loudness (arms glue, glue test) and Warmth (engages saturation, macro test) are asserted, and now Width and Clarity too: their MacroMap values (Width: stereo engaged, width 1 → 1.6 and space 0 → 0.35 from 40 %, monotonic, ungoverned, clamped at 2; Clarity: Clarity and Dynamic EQ engaged, presence +0.8, air +0.7 from 20 %, de-mud +0.5 by 70 %, monotonic, ungoverned) and their audible direction through the chain (side / mid −12.0 / −9.8 / −7.2 dB at Width 0 / 50 / 100 %; a quiet 3.2 kHz tone +0 / +2.4 / +4.8 dB at Clarity 0 / 50 / 100 %; figures from 03 §14.8). The Music mode bands (de-harsh, air lift, de-boom) are still not asserted at chain level. The loudness-matched bypass test sets the Loudness macro but asserts nothing about its effect. The subjective goals (punch, warmth) are not measured; the listening-panel tuning is roadmap (07 item 1.4). Re-voices every factory preset with Punch (render diff: 21 presets move with E04 / E20; golden renders re-recorded). |

after the session the trim returns to 0 dB (E37)`<br>• `App UI: the virtualiser's ear on the 7.1 Game strip is estimated on the 7.1 game scene (E37)`<br>• `App UI: the A/B/X test hides a random bank behind X, scores the answers with the binomial p-value and puts the bank back (E37)`<br>• `App UI: in the A/B/X test Boost 0 and Boost 100 play within 1 LU of each other (E37)`<br>• `App UI: the A/B/X panel covers the whole window, plays and answers with keys, and closing puts the bank back (E37)` | Implemented and tested | All 10 modules can be bypassed. Nine sit in ModuleSlots (the gate slot exists only in the Quality profile); the virtualiser uses a 20 ms fold crossfade and has zero latency. The chain latency is fixed per profile (192 samples Balanced at 48 kHz, asserted exactly in the MixEngine test) whether modules are on or off. With everything bypassed, the output equals the input delayed by that latency within 1e-6 (asserted). Latency-profile changes are structural: while the device runs the app swaps engines with a 2 × 10 ms dip (R1.5).<br><br>**Test gaps:**<br>1. `Chain: latency per profile and constant under module bypass` only checks per-profile ordering, the < 5 ms / < 2.5 ms bounds and `needsReprepare`; constancy under bypass is shown indirectly by the all-bypassed and audition tests.<br>2. The ModuleSlot test uses a signal where wet == dry (and checks channel 0 only), so it proves alignment but would also pass with a hard switch; crossfade smoothness between differing paths is not asserted.<br>3. Match accuracy was asserted only where the ceiling cap is not involved (the hot-programme case of docs/11 E37 now covers the capped case, green on CI in run 16): with +10 dB drive on a programme peaking at −25 dBFS, the matched reference reads within 0.5 LU of the processed short-term loudness, is the input times one gain of 10 ± 0.5 dB (residual ≤ 1 % of peak), and unmatched bypass equals the delayed input within 1e-6. Two further tests assert the ceiling (short-term loudness > -60 LUFS and peak ≤ -1 dBFS; and, with kicks rising to 0 dBFS, sample peak ≤ ceiling and true peak ≤ ceiling + 0.15 dB in all three profiles). How far the cap leaves the reference below the processed loudness on hot programmes is measured (below), not asserted.<br>4. Clicks on an A/B bank switch and on global bypass are now asserted (`tests/test_protection_gaps.cpp`): on a 1 kHz sine, with bank B = +9 dB EQ at 1 kHz, presence, saturation, width 1.5 and +3 dB drive, the largest sample-to-sample step around every bank switch and matched / unmatched bypass toggle stays within 1.1 × the steady signal's own (measured 1.000–1.002, 03 §14.8). The `ComparisonMatcher` law (acquire, hold for the comparison, 10 s end, 2 dB/s release, only the louder side attenuated) is unit-tested in the same file (green on CI in run 16; it replaced the raise-only, ceiling-capped LoudnessMatch, which read 2–3 LU short on hot programme).<br><br>Scratch harnesses (not in the repo): an earlier run measured matched bypass within 0.01 LU of the processed output after a 6 s warm-up (Loudness macro 100 %), unmatched -9.9 to -10.0 LU, and a sample-step ratio of 1.000 for a bank switch to +12 dB EQ, an EQ module bypass and global bypass matched / unmatched. A re-run for this matrix reproduced a 0.00 LU match on programme peaking at -15.6 dBFS, but on programme peaking near -6 dBFS the matched bypass read 1.8 LU below the processed output, because a positive match is capped at (ceiling − held dry peak) by design (03 §14.5). The matched gain slews at 3 dB/s, so it is off right after a change. A CLI re-measurement at the current tree (pink noise + 55 Hz kicks of random level, ≈ −22.8 LUFS, Loudness macro 100 %, three seeds × three profiles) settled 1.2–1.5 LU below the processed output, with every bypass render at −1.05 dBTP / −1.05 dBFS.<br><br>The GUI A/B buttons, copy, Bypass with the loudness-matched menu and the ear hold-to-bypass are compiled in the CI `app` job (Linux headless screenshot) but have no automated interaction test. The plug-in has no A/B banks; it relies on the host.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 2 batch 2 (green in CI run 36391842718): the global bypass engaged with a click (the reference's true-peak limiter started cold, silent for its 67-sample latency, inside the crossfade); found by the E53 soak and fixed in review: the crossfade now waits that latency (`KnownGap closed: engaging the global bypass is click-free ...`). The reference limiter has the LF-safe envelope (E10: a hot 100 + 200 Hz reference −55.7 → −176.7 dBFS DC). |

from 1 (E06)` | Implemented and tested | Scope of the status: the strip's processed path (maximizer limiter plus attenuation-only output trim), the global-bypass reference (its own true-peak limiter, `dryLimiter`) and the MixEngine master limiter.<br><br>Re-verified at the current tree with a fresh Release build (not CI): 24/24 TruePeakLimiter, 20/20 LoudnessMaximizer, 16/16 `Chain:`, 3/3 MixEngine, 5/5 Factory presets, 3/3 TruePeakDetector and 7/7 DeviceProfiles pass.<br><br>Measured (03 §10.6; ideal 8× FFT reconstruction, 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz, 4 seeds per cell): sample peak at or below −0.0501 dB re ceiling in every case, safety clamp never engaged; worst true peak re ceiling −0.03 dB on noise band-limited to 0.39 fs, and −0.031 to −0.023 dB on noise band-limited to 0.45 fs.<br><br>Factory presets (03 §1.4; the author's instrumented renders, not a test assertion): 24 presets at their own settings and at full macros, 48 renders; worst −1.048 dBTP for a −1 dBTP ceiling (−2.05 dBTP for the −2 dBTP Bluetooth preset), no clamp. `LoudnessMaximizer.h` records −1.04 dBTP.<br><br>Thresholds the tests assert:<br>• limiter: true peak at most +0.1 dB on band-limited signals, +0.15 dB on the CD band and +2 dB on full-band signals; sample peak exact;<br>• chain and presets: true peak at most ceiling + 0.15 dB on the 4× meter, sample peak at or below the ceiling + 1e−6, clamp count 0.<br><br>Latency at 48 kHz (Low Latency / Balanced / Quality): strip limiter 44 / 92 / 116 samples; maximizer 60 / 128 / 152 samples; master limiter 68 samples at 1 ms, or 44 samples when every strip runs Low Latency (the MixEngine test asserts totals of 192+48+20 and 100+24+20). Limiter CPU 150–168 ns per stereo sample (03 §10.6).<br><br>Limitations:<br>• only the sample-peak bound is a proof; the true-peak bound is measured;<br>• raw full-band white noise ends 0.90 to 1.35 dB above the ceiling on ideal reconstruction;<br>• very hard clipping (18 dB drive) reaches +0.17 dB;<br>• a 0 ms look-ahead is available through the API only and has no true-peak guarantee;<br>• device ceiling cap: only `DeviceProfiles::adviceFor()` is unit-tested (−1 / −2 / −3 dBTP); the EngineController → `AudioEngineHost::setMasterCeilingDb` → MixEngine wiring has no test, the CI headless `--device` screenshot uses a USB profile whose cap equals the −1 dBTP default and asserts nothing about the ceiling, and real WASAPI, CoreAudio and Bluetooth devices are not exercised (device-lab plan in 10 §5);<br>• the plug-in has no master limiter; the CLI has none either, but with `--ceiling` or `--target-lufs` it applies an offline static trim when the rendered true peak exceeds the ceiling (maximizer on).<br><br>**Matched bypass: fixed and tested.** Before the bypass-reference limiter existed, the loudness-matched global bypass (Loudness macro 100 %) could exceed the ceiling: manual CLI renders of pink noise + 55 Hz kicks peaked at −0.13 to +0.28 dBFS under a −1 dBTP ceiling, and the programme of the new regression test (kicks rising to 0 dBFS) reached about +5.3 dBTP. The cause was that the per-block cap (`max.ceiling` − held dry peak) cannot pull the 50 ms gain ramp down in time when a new, louder dry peak arrives. The reference now passes `dryLimiter`, a TruePeakLimiter at `max.ceiling` (1 ms look-ahead + 20-sample detector = 68 samples at 48 kHz, taken out of the dry delay, so no latency is added), which runs only while bypass is engaged. *Chain: matched bypass never overshoots the ceiling when a louder dry peak arrives* asserts sample peak ≤ ceiling + 1e−6 and true peak ≤ ceiling + 0.15 dB from 100 ms after engaging, in Quality, Balanced and Low Latency. A CLI re-measurement at the current tree (three seeds × three profiles, plain `process` with `bypass=on`) read −1.05 dBTP / −1.05 dBFS in every render. The ceiling now holds in bypass in every host: app, plug-in and CLI.<br><br>Hardening wave A (green on CI in run 16): the governor's limiter gain-reduction input is taken per fixed 10 ms window, so it no longer depends on the host block size, and a module switched off mid-stream publishes no stale gain reduction or clip energy.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 2 batch 1 (green in CI run 36367474107): the chain splits each host block on the governor's 10 ms grid (E06), and a finite sample beyond +24 dBFS is muted, counted and hidden from the control loops (E10).<br><br>docs/11 Phase 2 batch 2 (green in CI run 36391842718): an automatic preamp (`auto.preamp`, off by default; E11) takes the chain's predicted static boost minus an allowance (1 dB) off the signal ahead of the modules; on a hot master the limiter works more than 1 dB 17.9 → 7.5 % of the time (Signature) and 49.3 → 6.9 % (Punchy Pop), still over the ≤ 2 % target (KnownGap). The app shows the preamp and the limiter's active share (LoudnessPanel `PRE`, `LIM`).<br><br>docs/11 Phase 3 batch 1 (green in CI run 36521915726): the new stages (Startle Guard, Smoothness, loudness contour) all sit before the maximizer, so the true-peak limiter still sets the ceiling; the review rendered an 8 s stress programme (hot kicks, sibilant bursts, an explosion, full-scale square bursts; stereo and 7.1 at 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz) with every new stage on, Boost and all macros 100, 12 dB base drives, protection Off / Normal / Strict, Music and Gaming, blocks 64 / 1000 / 4096: every output read −1.05 dBTP against the −1 dBTP ceiling, with no discontinuity (`analyze --glitches`).<br><br>Phase 3 batch 4 (local only; core only, no app yet): the hearing guard's dose matches a hand computation, its cap holds every 5 s A-weighted Leq under the cap, and it is bit-identical off or with an unknown sensitivity (every shipped profile is unknown); the per-ear stage keeps a hard-panned source's ILD within 0.03 dB of the static per-ear gain through the whole chain at Boost 100, true peak at most -0.05 dB re the ceiling with a +15 dB profile, bit-identical without a profile.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 3 batch 5 (local, not yet on CI): the automatic preamp's prediction is within 1 dB of the rendered pink transfer maximum for all 30 factory presets at Boost 0 / 50 / 100 at -24 dBFS (59 -> 90 of 90); a -17 LUFS classical stand-in keeps its LRA within 0.1 LU with `auto.preamp`; a re-prepare at the same rate and layout keeps the governor's learned state. Known gaps re-based: Classical & Jazz on the -10.5 LUFS master 1.42 -> 0.72 LU (preamp) and 0.97 -> 0.44 LU (with `auto.preampHot`), both still over the <= 0.3 LU row. |

Podcast & Voice, no leveller oscillation above 1 dB (docs/11 E21)` | Implemented and tested | Restored to "Implemented and tested" at the current tree. The three reasons it had been lowered are resolved: the matched-bypass ceiling defect is fixed and tested (below, R3.1); AutoDrive is tested; and matching accuracy is asserted.<br>• AutoDrive: with +16 dB drive on a programme about 7 LU over a −18 LUFS target (Low Latency, maximizer only), the reduction never goes positive or below −24 dB and never moves faster than 2 dB/s, runs at exactly that limit while far over the target (−6 ± 0.3 dB between 2 s and 5 s), comes down to within 0.75 LU of the target within 10 s and never climbs back above that, and settles between −19 and −17.25 LUFS short-term with at least 3 dB of drive left; below the target it stays exactly 0 and the output is bit-identical to AutoDrive off; with a target the undriven programme already exceeds, the applied drive floors at 0 dB and the output is the delayed input within 1e-6; with +6 dB drive and an unreachable −24 LUFS target the reduction stops at −6 dB (within 1e-3) instead of running on towards −24 dB, and lowering the drive to 2 dB lifts it to ≥ −2 dB at once.<br>• matched bypass (then LoudnessMatch): where the ceiling cap is not involved (+10 dB drive, programme peaking at −25 dBFS), the matched reference reads within 0.5 LU of the processed output and is the input times one gain of 10 ± 0.5 dB; unmatched bypass is the delayed input within 1e-6.<br>• CLI `--target-lufs` loop: lands within 0.3 LU of the target, raising `max.drive` for a quiet file and lowering `output.gain` for a loud one, and is skipped with a warning (one pass) for `bypass=on` and for silence (R5.1).<br>Untested sub-parts: AutoLevel's convergence to its target (only slew and gating are asserted) and how far the ceiling cap leaves the matched reference short on hot programmes (measured below).<br><br>What the tests prove:<br>• LoudnessMeter: EBU Tech 3341 cases 1–6 within ±0.1 LU; Tech 3342 LRA within ±0.1 LU (the test checks ±1.0 and then ±0.1); histogram gating within 0.002 LU (integrated) and 0.02 LU (LRA) of a brute-force reference at 44.1–192 kHz.<br>• AutoLevel: 1 s of a −30 dBFS tone gives 0 < gain ≤ 1.05 dB, then a ±0.3 dB hold over 4 s of silence. Convergence to the target is not checked.<br>• Matched-bypass chain tests: one asserts match accuracy where the cap is not involved (above); one asserts only `shortTermLufs` > −60 and a channel-0 sample peak ≤ dbToGain(−1) + 1e−3 (linear) in the second second, on a quiet programme (`makeProgramme` level 0.2); one asserts the ceiling (sample and true peak, both channels, three profiles) with kicks rising to 0 dBFS.<br>Re-verified at the current tree: 32/32 LoudnessMeter, 2/2 LoudnessFollower, 1/1 AutoLevel and 16/16 `Chain:` pass in a fresh Release build.<br><br>Design values, all confirmed in `Protection.h/.cpp`:<br>• AutoLevel: ±12 dB, +1 dB/s up and −4 dB/s down, target −30…−10 LUFS (default −18);<br>• AutoDrive: reduce-only, 0 … −requested drive (at most −24 dB), min(2, 0.5·\|error\|) dB/s, 0.5 LU dead band, target −24…−6 LUFS (default −14);<br>• LoudnessMatch (replaced by `ComparisonMatcher`, R2.10, green on CI in run 16): ±12 dB at 3 dB/s, positive gain capped at `max.ceiling` − held dry peak, 50 ms gain ramp, then `dryLimiter` at `max.ceiling` (1 ms look-ahead, 80 ms auto release; `ProcessingChain.cpp`);<br>• gate: −70 dBFS RMS, −50 LUFS and 20 LU.<br><br>Manual CLI runs (fresh Release `flubsound-cli`; synthetic 12 s pink noise + 55 Hz kick at −24.67 LUFS, 48 kHz; similar to, not identical with, an earlier review's input):<br>• `--target-lufs -14 --ceiling -1`: −14.03 LUFS and −1.05 dBTP after 2 passes, with `max.drive` at 10.67 dB (earlier review: −14.00 LUFS, −1.50 dBTP);<br>• Loudness macro at 100 %: −14.79 LUFS; with `max.autoDrive=on` and `max.target=-18` it gave −16.73 LUFS integrated, AutoDrive still converging at the end of 12 s (earlier review: −14.67 and −16.72);<br>• matched bypass (`bypass=on`, Loudness at 100 %), before the bypass-reference limiter: −15.58 LUFS against −14.79 processed; the ceiling cap limited the matching (needed gain 9.9 dB, cap about 8.8 dB), so the reference stayed about 0.8 LU short;<br>• matched bypass at the current tree, on a different synthetic input (pink noise + 55 Hz kicks of random level, ≈ −22.8 LUFS, peaks −5.2 to −6.3 dBFS; three seeds, all three profiles): the settled reference (from 4 s) read 1.2–1.5 LU below the processed output, again limited by the cap, and every bypass render peaked at −1.05 dBTP / −1.05 dBFS.<br><br>**Matched-bypass ceiling: fixed.** Earlier manual runs found the reference exceeding the ceiling (−0.13 to +0.28 dBFS sample peak under −1 dBTP, first overshoot at 3.51 s) because the per-block cap could not pull the 50 ms gain ramp down when a new, higher dry peak arrived. The reference now passes a true-peak limiter at the ceiling inside the chain latency (details in R3.1), so the desktop app, the plug-in and a plain CLI `process` all hold the ceiling in bypass; the CLI's offline trim is no longer needed for it.<br><br>Known limits (03 §14.9 and §6.3): AutoDrive only reduces drive, the gates are absolute, and the saturator has no automatic make-up. A loudness target in the app's batch UI is roadmap (07 item 2.10).<br><br>Hardening wave A (green on CI in run 16): a programme more than 20 LU below the previous one was ignored by GatedLoudness for good; it is now held out for 3 s and the slow measure restarts, so AutoLevel converges and AutoDrive releases; the slow measure's cold-start bias is corrected. docs/11 E37: the matched bypass now reads within 0.5 LU on hot programme (was 2–3 LU short). AutoLevel's convergence to its target is now asserted through the restart case.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 3 batch 1 (green in CI run 36521915726): docs/11 E32: the relative loudness contour (`contour.on`, off by default; ISO 226:2023 lift for the playback level below a reference, with its own headroom trim, `ProcessingChain::setListeningLevelDb` for the host's endpoint offset) and `platform::AudioEndpoints::queryOutputVolume` (Linux tested against a fake `pactl`; Windows and macOS compiled only); the app does not feed it yet (`Contour: ...` 7 cases, `App: the Linux endpoint volume ...` 2 cases). docs/11 E37 / E40: the preset browser's preview is loudness matched against the current sound by turning the louder side down (`App UI: a louder preview is matched within 1 LU of the current sound 1 s after the flip (E37/E40)`, `App UI: going back from a quieter preview ...`, `App UI: matchTrims turns only the louder side down (E37)`).<br><br>Phase 3 batch 3 (local only): Late Night Low Volume within 1 LU of −20 LUFS at −14 / −24 / −40 LUFS (music and speech) and Podcast & Voice's speech rows met (both re-voiced); Auto Level's measure is 15 s, 5× AutoDrive's; the 10-minute mixed programme (`ctest -C Soak`) meets the 1 dB oscillation row on programme and misses it in combat by 0.05–0.08 dB (a KnownGap). |

CPU at 2× Low: Tape 143 ns, Tube 117 ns, Digital 58 ns.<br><br>Limitations:<br>• harder drive still aliases at 44.1 kHz (24 dB drive: Tape −28.0, Digital −35.5 dBc; pinned as a KnownGap in `tests/test_signal_hygiene.cpp`); 8× or ADAA is roadmap (08 B3, docs/11 E10);<br>• there is no automatic make-up: Tape at 9 dB drive loses 3.6 dB RMS on −12 dBFS pink noise;<br>• the tape head bump is a simplified constant-Q bell.<br><br>Usage: Gaming macros never engage saturation (no Sat entries in `kGamingTable`). No factory preset selects Tube or Digital: the two presets that set `sat.on` (`music-lo-fi-chill`, `music-warm-vinyl`) use the default Tape, and `presets/` contains no `sat.type` key. Tube and Digital are reachable only through the ModuleCard Type control, the plug-in parameters or `--set sat.type=`. |

after it that ducks the loud vowels 1.5 dB more than the "s" is taken back too` | Partially implemented | Why the row stays Partially implemented: the anti-clipping half is implemented and well tested on the processed path, and the THD protection now acts on a **measured** THD+N instead of the clip-energy proxy (03 §14.5, 9 of the 18 `Distortion:` cases), but its coverage is still limited.<br>• It takes back only governed macro contributions. Base `max.drive` and `sat.drive` (up to 24 dB each) are the user's or preset's choice and are never touched, so saturator THD set that way (about 40 % at 24 dB drive) is measured and published (`MeterBus::distortionDb`, the Distortion bar of `LoudnessPanel`) but not limited; it holds the governor at its floor instead.<br>• Only the saturator and the maximizer's soft clipper feed the governor. The bass harmonics generator and the air exciter are now measured as well (a two-reference estimate; 8 new `Distortion:` cases, green on CI in run 16), but are deliberately kept out of the governor input and the THD+N meter: they add harmonics on purpose, and fed to the governor at any weight they would hold it at its 0.3 floor on most factory presets that use harmonic bass (03 §14.5 has the table; an author measurement, not asserted). Their combined reading (`DistortionMonitor::getSmoothedHarmonicsDb()`) is not published on `MeterBus` or shown. Dynamics gain modulation is not measured (the limiter's is covered by the GR budget).<br>• At the clipper the governor still sees the clip energy ratio (over the same 25 ms window) whenever it reads higher than the measured THD+N, as it does on a steady tone: that floor keeps the governor from acting later than it did on the proxy alone (a real difference: on the protection-test programme at Boost 100 %, the THD+N alone would have left three factory presets with a higher scale than the proxy did; with the floor every preset's scale is at or below the proxy's, and Club Loud at its own settings now backs off to 0.39 instead of 0.92; 03 §14.5).<br>• The measured-THD+N cases are green on CI in run 14; the harmonic-generator cases added since are green on CI in run 16.<br><br>Re-verified at the current tree: 20/20 LoudnessMaximizer, 1/1 `SafetyGovernor:`, 2/2 `Protection:`, 17/17 `Distortion:`, 20/20 BassEngine, 22/22 Saturator and 2/2 Transparency pass in a fresh Release build.<br><br>Anti-clipping chain:<br>• an oversampled soft clipper (4× High in Quality and Balanced, 2× Low in Low Latency) feeds the true-peak limiter, which feeds a counted clamp;<br>• the clip threshold t sits 0.3–6 dB above the ceiling, and the curve never exceeds t; at the defaults t = +2.15 dBFS and the knee starts at −0.35 dBFS;<br>• the saturator runs at 2× oversampling;<br>• the bass shelf is withdrawn when its predicted LF peak would exceed `bass.protect`.<br><br>SafetyGovernor (`Protection.cpp`, all values confirmed):<br>• it watches 3 s averages of the limiter's block-minimum GR (budget −6 dB) and of the distortion input (budget −30 dB, about 3.2 % RMS): the saturator's measured THD+N power-summed with the clipper's, the latter floored at its clip energy ratio over the same 25 ms window;<br>• over budget, its scale falls 15 %/s to a floor of 0.3 (1 → 0.3 takes 4.7 s);<br>• once above −4.5 dB GR and below −31.5 dB distortion, it recovers at 3 %/s (0.3 → 1 takes 23 s);<br>• the GR test feeds −12 dB GR with −40 dB clip energy for 6 s, asserts 0.3 ≤ scale < 0.6, then asserts that the scale rises;<br>• the clip-energy unit test (its values now enter the distortion input) holds GR at 0 dB: −20 dB clip energy lowers the scale 0.15 ± 0.002 per second to exactly 0.3, −31 dB (inside the hysteresis) leaves it unchanged for 20 s, clean input releases it at 3 %/s once the average is under −31.5 dB, and the power-domain average trips on one 10 ms block at −3 dB but not at −15 dB;<br>• the chain test (base drive 10 dB + Boost 100 %, clipper share 1, 8 s of hot programme) trips the clip-energy budget while the mirrored 3 s GR average never goes below −4.5 dB (so the clip branch alone acts; clip energy peaks at about −11 dB), the scale reaches ≤ 0.35, every block's effective drive, bass boost, harmonics and saturation drive equal base + amount × the previous block's scale, presence, width, glue and the parameter store never move, and 20 s of quiet programme raise the scale again (0.30 → 0.55 measured).<br><br>Measured THD+N (`tests/test_distortion.cpp`):<br>• the estimator (per channel over a 25 ms window, residual after the least-squares gain relative to the output energy) equals a Goertzel harmonic analysis of a sine through tanh and through the Tube curve within 0.02 dB (measured < 0.001 dB) from −60 to −10 dB; linear gains read ≤ −90 dB, identity and silence exactly −160 dB;<br>• in the stages, the saturator's reading (Digital and Tape, 6–15 dB drive) and the 4× clipper's (0–12 dB drive) match a harmonic analysis of what the stage does within 0.1 dB (measured < 0.01 dB);<br>• the readings do not depend on the host block size: a 55 Hz tone through 12 dB of tape drive and through the clipper reads within 0.2 dB (measured < 0.05 dB) in 32- to 4096-sample blocks, where a per-block estimate read up to 14 dB low (mutation check);<br>• the monitor power-sums the two stages and smooths the meter with τ = 300 ms (±0.02 dB, independent of block size);<br>• the saturator's own reading 3–6 dB over the budget drives a governor to the 0.3 floor within 10 s, and 3–6 dB under it leaves the scale at 1;<br>• through the chain with the maximizer off (GR 0 dB, clip energy −160 dB), base tape drive 12 dB + Warmth 100 % on hot programme trips the governor (scale ≤ 0.35) and only Warmth's governed drive is scaled (every block within 1e−4), while Warmth 30 % on quiet programme stays ≥ 3 dB under the budget with the scale at exactly 1;<br>• through the chain with the maximizer alone on a steady 750 Hz sine whose clip energy is over the budget (−27.8 dB) and whose THD+N is under it (−31.1 dB), the chain's scale stays within 0.004 (one 25 ms window of fall) of a governor mirrored from the published GR and per-block clip energy, and both back off to ≤ 0.6 (with the floor removed the chain's scale stayed at 1; mutation check);<br>• measuring, the monitor and the governor update make 0 allocations (type crossfade included).<br><br>Measured (03 §11.3.5; noise band-limited to 0.4 fs, 4× High, 1.5 ms look-ahead, glue disarmed): 6 dB drive: clip energy −96.8 dB, GR −1.17 dB, true peak −0.04 dB re ceiling; 12 dB drive: clip energy −22.7 dB (over budget); 18 dB drive: clip energy −8.6 dB, true peak +0.17 dB. The maximizer test asserts true peak ≤ +0.1 dB while clip energy ≤ −12 dB, and ≤ +0.5 dB beyond that; the sample peak is exact.<br><br>Limitations:<br>• the THD+N is measured around the curves, before band-limiting (it includes harmonics above the base-rate Nyquist that the downsampler removes, and the Tube bias DC that the 10 Hz blocker removes: slightly conservative), and, as a governor input, only at the saturator and the clipper;<br>• a factory-preset test requires `max.drive` to stay 0 because fixed drive is ungoverned;<br>• the bass protection's 10 ms attack lets an onset overshoot its cap by about 3–4 dB (the limiter catches it);<br>• ADAA is roadmap;<br>• the true-peak residuals of R3.1 apply.<br><br>The anti-clip guarantee now also covers the loudness-matched bypass reference, which skips the maximizer: it passes its own true-peak limiter at the ceiling in every host (R3.1, test *Chain: matched bypass never overshoots the ceiling when a louder dry peak arrives*). <br><br>Harmonic generators (`tests/test_distortion.cpp`, new): the bass harmonics' reading equals a harmonic analysis of the stage output within 0.05 dB (40 / 80 Hz, every character, with and without the fundamental replaced) and the air exciter's within 0.05 dB with its linear shelf taken out (also on the band's skirt, where a fit against the input alone reads high); linear settings read -160 dB; 32- and 4096-sample blocks read the same (within 0.2 dB); the monitor power-sums and smooths them (τ = 300 ms) apart from the THD+N; through the chain, harmonics far over the −30 dB budget leave the governor scale at exactly 1 and the THD+N meter at −160 dB, where a governor fed them backs off to its floor; measuring allocates nothing.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 2 batch 1 (green in CI run 36367474107): E06 slice - the governor ticks once per 10 ms window, reports its state and reason, and protection strength Off / Normal / Strict (a host setting, no UI yet) also governs the base drives at Normal / Strict. All Music macros 100 on a −12 dBFS 50 Hz sine still measure 20.0 % THD+N at Off and Normal and 6.0 % at Strict (the ≤ 3 % Done-when is open); the E59 quality suite (`flubsound-cli quality`) measures THD+N, IMD, MTND, ducking and kick timing.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 2 batch 2 (green in CI run 36391842718): E06 step 1: at protection strength Normal / Strict the maximizer's share of the governor input is at least its whole-stage residual, which reads the limiter's gain-modulation IMD (a 50 + 63 Hz two-tone through the limiter −35.7 dB where the clipper reads −160 dB); strength is chosen in the app (Settings › Processing, or the Boost panel's governor chip, which names state and reason) and `--protection` in the CLI. All Music macros 100 on 50 Hz still read 20.0 % at Normal: the rest is the bass harmonics generator's intended harmonics, so the ≤ 3 % row needs a harmonics policy.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 3 batch 1 (green in CI run 36521915726): docs/11 E06 Phase 3: at protection strength Normal / Strict the governor runs a measured loop on two spans' loudness-weighted, masking-limited residual (`WeightedResidual`), a feed-forward from the maximizer input's peaks, a PLR (dynamics) budget and a harmonics policy with its own scale on `bass.harmonics`; all Music macros 100 on a 50 Hz sine 20.0 → 1.5 % THD+N at Normal (Strict 6.0 → 1.6 %), Off unchanged and bit-identical (`KnownGap closed: all Music macros at 100 on a 50 Hz sine - THD+N <= 3 % at protection strength Normal ...`, `WeightedResidual: ...`, `Protection: ...` 2 cases, `Chain: ...` 3 cases in `tests/test_protection_measured.cpp`). docs/11 E07: a tonal-balance rule scales the macros' presence and air lifts at Normal / Strict (Gaming full stack 2–5 kHz tilt +4.06 → +1.26 dB, `KnownGap closed at protection strength Normal: Gaming full stack ...`).<br>Phase 3 batch 2 (docs/11 E06): the drive's fall is tied to the limiter's release (sag 4.29 → 0.61 dB on the stationary scene), the learned state survives `reset()` and the engine swap, and the readouts are on `MeterBus`, in `render.stats` and `quality`; E07's Smoothness follows the post-maximizer output (the sibilance rows at Boost 100 met with Smoothness 100, off by default). Still open: the 3-minute row on mastered music (LRA 3.95 LU at Normal), and the E60 listening panel for the budgets. |

The LUFS and true-peak measurement core is unit-tested on its own:<br>• Tech 3341 cases 1–6 within ±0.1 LU at 48 kHz, and case 1 also at 44.1, 96 and 192 kHz;<br>• Tech 3342 LRA within ±0.1 LU (48 kHz, plus 44.1 / 96 kHz in the adversarial case);<br>• histogram gating within 0.002 LU (I) and 0.02 LU (LRA) of a brute-force reference;<br>• a 997 Hz 0 dBFS sine in one channel reads −3.01 ± 0.01 LUFS;<br>• true peak: an fs/4 sine at 45° reads 0 dBTP within 0.15 dB while its sample peak is −3.01 dB, and 15–20 kHz sines read within ±0.05 dB at 44.1 and 48 kHz;<br>• the LoudnessMeter has zero reading latency and does not allocate; the whole in-chain metering path is allocation-free, and meters only read, so they add no audio-path latency.<br>All of these pass at the current tree (614/614 suite; LoudnessMeter 32/32, TruePeakDetector 3/3).<br><br>The views' data side is tested in `flub_app_tests` (green on CI in run 11, all three OSes) through the real path `AudioEngineHost::renderOffline` → `ProcessingChain` (MeterBus atomics, AnalyzerTaps rings) → `MeterSnapshot` / `AnalyzerFeed` → view:<br>• spectrum (4096-pt Hann FFT, 420 log points, 1/6-octave bands): a 1 kHz sine at 0 and −20 dBFS reads its level within ±0.5 dB at 44.1 and 48 kHz, the bands an octave away read at least 60 dB lower, and a −20 dBFS sine fed through a chain's pre tap and `AnalyzerFeed` reads −20 ± 0.5 dB;<br>• `AnalyzerFeed` hands every sink the same blocks and trims a stalled backlog to the newest 8192 samples;<br>• `LevelMeters`: a −6.02 dBFS sine shows a peak of −6.02 ± 0.05 dB and an RMS (LevelMeter, τ = 300 ms) 3.01 ± 0.05 dB below it on the input and output bars, and the output bar falls to −17 dB or lower within 1 s of silence;<br>• correlation: +1 and −1 within 0.01 for mono and anti-phase sines and 0 ± 0.1 for independent noise, in the snapshot and in the `LoudnessPanel` readout;<br>• `WaveformHistory`: 480 samples per column at 48 kHz (1200 columns over 12 s), the exact min / max of a sine and of silence, the short-term loudness stored per column, partial columns not committed, a non-finite loudness stored as silence, `reset()`;<br>• `LoudnessPanel` SHORT reads −20.0 ± 0.2 LUFS for a −20 dBFS 1 kHz sine on both channels.<br><br>Not tested: painting and layout (the spectrum path, meter colours, the IEC 60268-18 deflection scale), peak hold, and the `LoudnessPanel` momentary / integrated / LRA / true-peak readouts (their measurement core is tested above). The +0.3 dB (44.1 kHz) and +0.4 dB (48 kHz) reading of a 0 dBFS 1 kHz sine on the spectrum is a manual measurement recorded in 06 §6.4; the test only bounds it to ±0.5 dB.<br><br>Manual run in an earlier pass (app sources unchanged since): `--mode music` at 1440×900 loads the Bluetooth Headphones preset (ceiling −2 dBTP) and shows TRUE PEAK −2.1 dBTP and LATENCY 5.4 ms offline.<br><br>Documented limits (03 §13.9): the true-peak meter uses the plain 4× grid (no refinement), so short transients near fs/2 can read up to about 0.5 dB low; 03's grid formula cos(πf/4fs) gives −0.55 dB at 0.45 fs. Loudness readings update at 10 Hz. Indicative CPU (03 §13.6 / §15): LoudnessMeter 8–9 ns, TruePeakMeter 83–87 ns, LevelMeter 8.5–9.3 ns per stereo sample. |

bit-identical (E40)` | Implemented and tested | This status covers the preset library, the preset format and applying presets to the engine. It does not cover the app's preset-management UI (see below).<br><br>The library has 24 factory presets in three categories: 12 Music, 9 Gaming, 3 Device. That count is taken from the folder; the test only requires at least 20 files, exactly the three categories, and seven named presets. Each mode has 5 macros. Music: Punch, Width, Clarity, Loudness, Warmth. Gaming: Footsteps, Positional, Impact, Detail, Voice & Score. The format is versioned, key-based JSON (`"flubsound-preset"`, version 2 since docs/11 E01: a version-1 file without `virt.lfe` keeps its old 0 dB LFE default): unknown keys are ignored, out-of-range values are clamped, and choices are stored as labels.<br><br>The tests render every preset through the full ProcessingChain at 48 kHz in 512-sample blocks, for 4 s of hot programme (8-channel for surround presets), and require:<br>• sample peak ≤ ceiling (+1e-6);<br>• true peak ≤ ceiling + 0.15 dB;<br>• zero limiter safety-clamp engagements;<br>• output not silent (last 2 s RMS ≥ −40 dBFS);<br>• latency within its profile bound: Quality ≤ 40 ms, Balanced ≤ 5 ms, Low Latency ≤ 2.5 ms.<br>With Boost and all five macros at 100 %, the first 2 s still hold the ceiling (sample and true peak) with no safety clamps.<br><br>Also enforced: ceiling ≤ −1 dBTP (≤ −2 dBTP for Bluetooth); maximizer on; `output.gain` ≤ 0; `max.drive` = 0; no global bypass. Gaming presets must store no crossfeed and keep width ≤ 1.25 and space ≤ 0.2. Gaming presets named competitive or tournament must use Low Latency. The low-latency tag must match the Low Latency profile. Every preset a device profile suggests must exist. All 6 factory-preset cases pass at the current tree (614/614 suite).<br><br>Not covered by tests (JUCE code, build-verified only): app-side user-preset save / rename / delete / import / export, the modified dot, the tray preset submenu, the preset hotkeys and the plug-in import / export. Roadmap: preset browser UX hardening (07 item 1.3); cloud / community presets and game auto-profiles (09 §4).<br><br>docs/11 E40 (green on CI in run 16): loading a factory or user preset no longer changes the latency profile or bypass state (it used to switch profiles and force a re-prepare); a preset's profile is only a suggestion. Also in the plug-in.<br><br>Wave B2 (local runs only, green on CI in run 17): the app's preset loading now keeps app state too (docs/11 E40 app half: a user preset saved in Quality no longer switches every strip to Quality and re-prepares), and there are 25 factory presets: "Voice Chat" (docs/11 E23: speech at −35 / −12 LUFS ends 2.2–2.6 LU apart short-term, true peak ≤ −1.05 dBTP). A strip with no saved state starts from Signature (Music, System), Voice Chat (Chat) or "First Run – Game" (Competitive FPS with Boost 0.20, Footsteps 0.30, Detail 0.15: −50 / −60 dBFS pink beds +2.70 / +2.62 LU instead of +4.92 / +9.69 LU), docs/11 E36.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 2 batch 1 (green in CI run 36367474107): E52 Phase A and Phase B core - preset schema major.minor with a migration registry, a permanent uuid per factory preset and a contentHash, reported warnings (`bost` → did you mean "boost"?) in the CLI and the plug-in import, deterministic plug-in state recall (a missing parameter takes its default), and golden files for every default and every factory preset's uuid, hash and render (the render gated in CI on the gcc leg only). The app keys automatic-profile rules and each strip's last preset by the preset's uuid since docs/11 Phase 2 batch 2 (green in CI run 36391842718; the old ids migrate once through the alias map). Since docs/11 E19's cue enhancer the −50 / −60 dBFS pink beds rise +0.98 / +0.95 LU through First Run – Game and +2.5 LU through the shipped Competitive FPS (was +9.7 LU; `App: First Run - Game ...` now asserts ≤ 3 LU for both).<br><br>docs/11 Phase 2 batch 2 (green in CI run 36391842718): E52 app half: the app keys presets by uuid (strip presets and automatic-profile rules migrate once, settings schema 2), a user preset without one gets one written into its file, *Rename* keeps it, user presets are saved with every parameter, and reader warnings reach a toast; a damaged settings file is quarantined as `.corrupt-<time>` and the newest of three rotating backups restored (`tests/app/test_app_presets.cpp`, `test_app_settings.cpp`). New parameters (layout version 3): `auto.preamp`, `auto.preampAllowance`, `max.clipCrest`, `max.clipMaxDb`, `max.style`, `max.lfLimit`, `max.bedLift`, all defaulting to the previous sound; the golden files were re-recorded for them (every `contentHash` moves because it covers every parameter).<br><br>docs/11 Phase 3 batch 1 (green in CI run 36521915726): docs/11 E40: a preset browser (search over name, tags, category, mode, author and description; scope, mode, category and tag filters; favourites and recents by uuid; the description pane with reader warnings) with a plain preview in the active bank that Cancel restores bit for bit and Load commits through `EngineController` (9 `App UI:` cases in `tests/app/test_app_ui_preset_browser.cpp`, `App settings: favourite and recent presets are kept by id, recents newest first and at most 8 (E40)`); the separate audition bank and hover audition are open.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 3 batch 3 (local only): two factory presets re-voiced, as E21's Done-when requires: Late Night Low Volume (Auto Level −26 LUFS, a 10:1 compressor with +11.8 dB make-up, a stronger upward section; −40 / −24 / −14 LUFS music now within 1 LU of −20 LUFS) and Podcast & Voice (Auto Level −22 LUFS, 6:1 compressor, an upward section; speech spread 5.67 → 1.79 LU); their golden renders −21.86 → −19.64 and −16.49 → −13.10 LUFS. First Run – Game is Competitive FPS with only Boost capped at 0.20 (Footsteps 0.80 and Detail 0.30 as shipped): beds +0.50 / +0.48 LU, step / bed contrast up by about 3 dB against the old caps. A new key, `spatial.crossfeedType` (layout version 6, Bs2b by default), moves every `contentHash`.<br><br>Phase 3 batch 4 (local only): every factory preset states its measured intent and one case per preset asserts it (30 / 30, registered from the folder: `Factory presets: intent - <category> - <name> (docs/11 E14)`); 25 -> 30 factory presets (Rock & Metal, Orchestral & Film, Acoustic & Singer-Songwriter, R&B & Vocal, Electronic & Ambient; perception not yet checked by a panel); Classical & Jazz loses 1.42 LU of LRA on a -10.5 LUFS master (KnownGap, row <= 0.3). A save or an A/B copy during a preview takes the pre-preview sound. |
