The icon is redrawn on MasterEnable changes, and a left click opens the window on Windows and Linux (macOS always shows the menu).<br><br>Global hotkeys work on Windows (`RegisterHotKey` + `MOD_NOREPEAT`), macOS (Carbon), Linux under X11 (`XGrabKey`) and Linux Wayland sessions through the xdg-desktop-portal GlobalShortcuts interface (tested against a mock portal only) for 6 actions:<br>• Enable / Disable: Ctrl+Alt+F<br>• Toggle Music / Gaming: Ctrl+Alt+M<br>• Boost ±10 %: Ctrl+Alt+Up / Down<br>• Next / Previous preset: Ctrl+Alt+Right / Left<br>Chords can be rebound by the user. Each action is registered under its name (shown by the Wayland portal's dialog), and its status (registered, in use, declined by the desktop, bound by the desktop as another key, waiting for the desktop, off, not supported) is shown on its row of the Hotkeys page; failures known at start-up are printed to stderr, and feedback appears in a tray bubble. The arrow-key defaults register only since the shared validator accepts navigation keys; before that they failed on every OS.<br><br>Start with the OS (roadmap 1.5): *Settings › General › Start Flubsound Pro when I sign in* writes the per-user `Run` registry value on Windows, registers `SMAppService.mainAppService` on macOS 13+ (hidden on older macOS) and an XDG autostart entry on Linux; the switch reads the OS entry back whenever the page is shown. Only the Linux path is tested (and was run end to end); the Windows code has been compiled with MinGW but never run, and the macOS code has not been compiled against a real SDK or run.<br><br>**Missing:** a run of the Wayland portal hotkeys on a real desktop (KDE Plasma 5.27+, GNOME 48+, Hyprland). Only a mock portal on a private bus is tested; `parent_window` is empty and no `.desktop` file is installed, so how each desktop identifies the app is untested; shortcuts the user declines, the desktop leaves unbound or binds to another key are now shown per action on the Hotkeys page, tested against the mock portal and with a fake service only (green on CI in run 16); triggers are compared by English key names, so a localised `trigger_description` shows as another key. Also missing: a run of start-with-the-OS on Windows and macOS. On Linux, close-to-tray minimises the window instead, because a tray host is not guaranteed.<br><br>The chord validation shared by Windows and macOS is unit-tested only in the Linux build of `flub_tests`. Linux X11 hotkeys are tested under Xvfb, Wayland hotkeys against a mock portal; Windows / macOS hotkey and all tray behaviour is build-verified only, and the repo records no manual verification. macOS 15 rejects Option-only and Option+Shift chords (`platform/macos/README.md`).<br><br>After run 14: `unregisterHotkey` on X11 now waits for the server (`XSync`, not `XFlush`), so a released chord is free when it returns; CI run 36305160127 (ASan job) had failed the X11 case on exactly that race (green on CI in run 16 since the fix).<br><br>Wave B2 (local runs only, green on CI in run 17): 11 hotkey actions instead of 6 (docs/11 E56 Phase A). Strip actions go to the active automatic profile's strip, else the Settings › Hotkeys strip (default Game), never the GUI selection, and the feedback names it ("Game: Boost 60%"); Focus latches Footsteps at 100 %, Night the Night Mode dynamics, ChatMix moves Game and Chat by ±1.2 dB per press up to ±6 dB, Bypass bypasses that strip only. The Wayland portal probe no longer blocks start-up (at most 250 ms instead of 3 s; a late or later portal is used), a lost session bus is reported, answers for an outdated batch or a closed session are not shown as the outcome, and GNOME's and German / French / Spanish / Brazilian Portuguese trigger descriptions count as registered (docs/11 E41; a GNOME shortcut without a key is Declined). X11: releasing the modifiers first no longer swallows the next press, and a chord bound to another id is refused. macOS: two hot-key services no longer fire each other's callbacks (checked by reading; the `.mm` is not compiled on this machine). No OSD yet.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 3 batch 5 (local, not yet on CI): hotkey (and `ctl`) actions show a click-through, never-focused on-screen display for 1.2 s (hidden in Tournament mode; an optional earcon in exclusive fullscreen); `flubsound-cli ctl <action> [strip] [value]` drives the running app over a per-user socket. The Windows window styles and socket are checked by MinGW-w64 only; the in-game PresentMon check is gated on the owner's PC. |

ParameterStore, A/B banks, preset, mode and latency profile.<br><br>Tested (all pass at the current tree; MixEngine 4/4, Platform 20/20, DriftFifo 5/5, `flub_app_tests` AppRouting 7/7 and the automatic-profile cases 7/7):<br>• stores survive re-configure;<br>• strips are summed and master-limited to −1 dBFS sample peak / −0.85 dBTP true peak with 0 allocations and no safety clamps;<br>• latency at 48 kHz, as reported by `getLatencySamples()`: Balanced 192+48+20 = 260 samples (5.4 ms), Low Latency 100+24+20 = 144 samples (3.0 ms), Quality 1352+48+20 = 1420 samples (29.6 ms); the impulse-position check confirms the Balanced and Low Latency values only, and Quality is asserted on the reported latency alone;<br>• per-app capture FIFO: ±200 / ±2000 ppm drift settles with no xruns after settling, fill within ±1 ms of target, correction within 5 ppm of the true drift, 0 allocations;<br>• the routing model (`AppRouting` with its real worker thread and message-thread hand-off, a fake router and fake captures; `flub_app_tests`, green on CI in run 11): executables matched to strips (path, case and `.exe` ignored), one endpoint move per process, the OS error shown on the app, un-mapping moving the app back to the default endpoint, shutdown restoring only the endpoints it moved, Automatic resolving to endpoint routing or process capture, a failing capture retried and then given up with its error still shown (the error used to vanish after the last retry; fixed in this pass), a running capture re-pointed on re-mapping, captures stopped when the app exits or routing is disabled, and routes and method persisted through `AppSettings`.<br><br>Routing status per OS:<br>• **Linux** works through `pactl move-sink-input` into the `flubsound_*` null sinks. JSON parsing, pid handling and argument sanitising are unit-tested. Real moves are not run in CI, which has no sound server.<br>• **Windows**: session enumeration and per-process loopback capture (build ≥ 19041, Windows 10 2004, as OBS gates it; Microsoft documents 20348; not yet run on a 19041–19045 machine; cannot mute the original stream) are implemented. Programmatic endpoint moves exist only in opt-in `FLUB_ENABLE_UNDOCUMENTED_ROUTING` builds, are not validated per Windows build (07 item 2.4), and need the unbuilt virtual driver to be useful. Otherwise the UI opens `ms-settings:apps-volume`.<br>• **macOS**: not supported (process taps are roadmap 3.2).<br><br>RoutingPanel's routing rows, the 2 s timer (the tests start passes with `refresh()`) and all Windows routing / capture code have no automated tests, and real OS routing cannot be exercised in CI.<br><br>Automatic profiles (roadmap 2.5, 09 §4 game auto-profiles; round 8, green on CI in run 16): while a rule's application is in the foreground, its strip plays the rule's preset (optionally in Music or Gaming mode), and with *restore on exit* both banks of the strip's previous state return when the application leaves. Asserted with a scripted foreground source: 2 stable polls (1 s) before any switch, a one-poll alt-tab ignored, Flubsound's own window and unanswered polls hold the state, one rule at a time (End before Apply), first match by path, name or bundle id, a manual preset change cancels the rule until the app has left (also through edits of other rules, fixed in this review), edited rules end without restoring, a deleted preset's error shown until the app leaves, rules persisted and trimmed like `AppSettings` reads them, the routing panel's list, its unsupported reason and the add form. Foreground detection: the X11 path is tested under Xvfb against a real server (own window, another process, a window without `_NET_WM_PID`, a destroyed window's BadWindow swallowed); Wayland reports itself unsupported (no portable foreground API). The Windows code passes a MinGW-w64 syntax check with the warning flags as errors and the macOS code was checked by reading; neither has run, and no rule has been tried against a real game.<br><br>Hardening wave B part 1 (green on CI in run 16): endpoint routes of apps that exited while routed are undone (in the same run and after a restart), a given-up capture is retried after a re-mapping, a method change or a stopped capture, a worker pass computed from an outdated configuration is dropped, and a reused process id is not mistaken for the routed process.<br><br>Wave B2 (docs/11 E47a; local runs only, green on CI in run 17): a Windows build without `FLUB_ENABLE_UNDOCUMENTED_ROUTING` lists applications but cannot move them, so *Automatic* resolves to process capture or to Disabled with a reason, never to endpoint routing whose every move fails (tested with a fake router whose `isSupported()` still claims support). The routing panel shows a red *No apps are being processed* notice (colour, outline, `!` and title; also its accessible description) whenever no application is routed or captured, except while nothing is assigned and the device input feeds a strip.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 2 batch 1 (green in CI run 36367474107): E48a - on Linux the router requests a 256/48000 PipeWire quantum and links each mapped sink's monitor ports to the device inputs with `pw-link` (read from `pw-dump`), re-checked every 10 s; tested against recorded `pw-dump` output and a fake `pw-link`, not on a real PipeWire system.<br>Phase 3 batch 2 (docs/11 E47 / E55): the doubling guard, a route journal replayed after a crash (a real SIGKILL / `TerminateProcess` of a child process is tested), a process-info cache and Tournament mode's engine side, all tested with fakes; the Windows code has not run on Windows.<br><br>Phase 3 batch 3 (local only): Tournament mode switches itself on while a known anti-cheat service runs and back to the user's choice 20 s after it stops; while on, no session enumeration, process open or foreground query (counted with fakes). The service query has still not run on Windows.<br><br>Phase 3 batch 4 (local only): with *Duck game under voice chat* on (off by default), speech on the Chat strip dips the Game and Music strips 1 - 4 kHz by 3 - 6 dB (footstep bands within 0.5 dB) and holds Game peaks under the master ceiling: -1 dBTP explosions move -20 LUFS chat 0.20 -> 0.02 dB short-term (-14 LUFS chat 1.02 -> 0.82 dB, not met); ChatMix is one complementary balance from the tray flyout, the Chat row and the hotkeys; off and centred are bit-identical. Not played against Sonar with real Discord captures.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 3 batch 5 (local, not yet on CI): Linux route journal: after kill -9 and a restart a `pw-play` stream is back on the default output and WirePlumber does not route the app to the Game sink again (headless PipeWire 1.0 + WirePlumber 0.4; 20 / 20 runs here); the routing panel shows the node's link status. |

It is the user / kernel ABI (control block, IOCTL codes, endpoint ids). There is no driver code, and no app / engine code includes it; only `tests/test_driver_shared.cpp` and `tests/test_driver_shared_c.c` do.<br>macOS: none (`platform/macos/README.md` design only).<br>Linux: `platform/linux/flubsound-pipewire-setup.sh` (install / remove / status / print-pa-config), `platform/linux/pipewire/pipewire.conf.d/90-flubsound-sinks.conf` (`support.null-audio-sink` nodes), `platform/linux/pipewire/pipewire-pulse.conf.d/90-flubsound-app-routing.conf`<br>Third-party cable on any OS: `app/Source/engine/EngineController.cpp` (`looksLikeLoopbackDevice`, `applyDeviceInputPolicy`, including the multi-strip `deviceInputMap` setting), `app/Source/engine/AudioEngineHost.*` (`setDeviceInputMap`: device-input → strip map), `app/Source/settings/AppSettings.*` (`DeviceInputMode` Automatic / On / Off, `deviceInputMap`), `app/Source/ui/SettingsDialog.*` (Device input, Input feeds strip) | `tests/test_driver_shared.cpp`:<br>• `Driver ABI: shared header constants, magic byte order and IOCTL codes`<br>• `Driver ABI: ring index maths over a non power-of-two buffer and 64-bit positions`<br>• `Driver ABI: Generation sequence lock rejects copies during and across a change`<br>• `Driver ABI: the C compiler lays the shared structs out like C++` (uses `tests/test_driver_shared_c.c`, compiled as strict C89 on gcc / clang only via `FLUB_TEST_DRIVER_SHARED_C`; skipped on MSVC)<br>These prove the ABI header only. **No test covers the Linux sink script or PipeWire drop-ins, the device-input cable detection or mapping, or any virtual driver (none exists).** | Partially implemented | What exists today:<br>1. Linux PipeWire / PulseAudio null sinks `flubsound_game` (7.1), `flubsound_music`, `flubsound_chat` and `flubsound_system`, created by the idempotent script or a PipeWire drop-in. The engine reads a sink's monitor only through its ordinary device input. Settings maps that input to one strip ("Input feeds strip"). Feeding several strips needs the `deviceInputMap` settings key (e.g. `Game=0;Music=8`), which has no UI. Connecting the monitors to that input is not automated. None of this is exercised in CI.<br>2. Any third-party virtual cable (VB-Cable, VoiceMeeter, BlackHole, a JACK port) can feed a strip through the device input. In Automatic mode an input is used only if its name contains flubsound, cable output, vb-audio, voicemeeter, blackhole, soundflower or loopback. "Stereo Mix" and "Monitor of" are excluded on purpose to avoid feedback. Build-verified only.<br><br>Designed only (roadmap):<br>• Windows WaveRT driver "Flubsound Virtual Audio": Game 7.1, Music, Chat and System render endpoints plus Mic, zero-copy IOCTL buffer mapping, clock slaving; milestones M1–M5; roadmap 2.1–2.3; EV / attestation signing per 08 D6. Only the ABI header exists; its layout, ring maths and sequence lock are tested (4/4 pass at the current tree).<br>• macOS Audio Server Plug-in via libASPL (roadmap 3.1): no code.<br>• Native PipeWire `pw_filter` node: roadmap 3.5 / 09 §3.<br><br>Driver-path latency figures are estimates, not measurements, and the docs now share one budget (01 §5): the driver README §6 uses the same rows and figures as 01 §5.2, ≈9.5 ms added in Low Latency (app engine 100 + 24 + 20 = 144 samples = 3.0 ms, as the MixEngine test asserts) and ≈12–13 ms in Balanced, and lists what lies outside it (the application's own path, classic 10 ms periods, the non-zero-copy capture). 06 §14 now has an R4.6 row. |

The worker is waited on explicitly (the timeout is only a hang guard). Not tested: the dialog's painting, the file choosers and Reveal. Limits: one file at a time (the CLI's `--jobs N` is parallel), no progress within a file, no resampling.<br><br>Hardening wave A (green on CI in run 16): the export job can be aborted inside decode, analysis and encode; hidden and `._` files are skipped and the nested-output check uses canonical paths (symlinks); the CLI reads UTF-8 preset paths on Windows, reaches quiet loudness targets through `input.gain` and shortens long UTF-8 names on a code-point boundary; the tests use per-process temporary folders. |

Upgraded from "verified by build / manual run only": that glue now has automated tests (green on CI in run 9). Asserted: a render equals the chain run by hand, bit for bit at every block size, sample-aligned (bypassed modules give the input back sample for sample, first and last frames included); `process` writes float32 / PCM24 / PCM16 files that read back in that format, at the input rate and length, in stereo, float32 bit-identical to the render and PCM within 1.5 LSB; the output report equals `analyze` of the written file (for a −100 dBFS programme in PCM16 the dither lifts its RMS by more than 3 dB over the float render's); a write failure is reported; a missing input exits 1 and output = input exits 2. The static ceiling trim is not exercised by these tests.<br><br>Manual checks for this matrix:<br>• a `bypass=on` render wrote 288000 of 288000 frames with cross-correlation lag 0 (sample-aligned) and gain 0.9989 (loudness-matched bypass, not bit-exact by design); re-checked with a different file: 384000 / 384000 frames, lag 0, gain 0.9988;<br>• a 7.1 input exported to stereo in the Low Latency profile (latency 100 smp = 2.08 ms compensated) at −16.00 LUFS;<br>• `--boost 100 --set max.drive=24 --ceiling -1` exported at TP −1.05 dBTP (the re-check read −1.01 dBTP);<br>• the Quality profile reported 1352 smp (28.17 ms) compensated (re-checked).<br><br>The printed and `--json` output report describes the file as written: a PCM16 / PCM24 export is read back and analysed, so quantisation and dither are included (float32 round-trips bit-exactly, so the render's analysis is the file's). 03 §1.4 measured the 24 factory presets at full macros at no more than −1.048 dBTP for a −1 dBTP ceiling (−2.05 dBTP for the −2 dBTP Bluetooth preset).<br><br>In the app (new in this round, green on CI in run 16): the Export / batch process dialog writes WAV through the same `writeRender` (a WAV export is byte-identical to the CLI renderer's output for the same WAV and parameters, float32 and PCM16, asserted) and FLAC 24 / 16 through JUCE with the same dither (at a loudness target within 0.3 LU under the ceiling, measured on the file, asserted).<br><br>Limits: the desktop app cannot record its processed output live. The CLI exports WAV only (float32 / PCM24 / PCM16; no PCM32 / float64 output option); the app adds FLAC 24 / 16; there is no MP3 / Ogg / AAC output. CLI output is always stereo. The DAW-bounce route through FlubsoundFX is neither documented as an export path nor tested (the plug-in has no tests; CI only builds it).<br><br>Hardening wave A (green on CI in run 16): a FLAC encoder that fails while finishing now fails the file, and a partly written WAV is removed on non-ASCII Windows paths too.<br><br>Phase 3 batch 3 (local only): `flubsound-cli demo` writes 24 before / after WAV pairs matched within 0.5 LU (level features left unmatched and named), with band deltas equal to `analyze` and byte-identical output across runs and worker counts.<br><br>docs/11 Phase 3 batch 5 (local, not yet on CI): `flubsound-cli demo` grows from 24 to 61 loudness-matched pairs, one for every entry of the new feature and listening guide (docs/12), which a test keeps in step with the pair list. |

ONNX Runtime DirectML / CoreML / CPU and chain integration still roadmap)<br>[09 §1.2 Candidate neural features (prioritised)](09-future-roadmap.md#12-candidate-neural-features-prioritised) (N1–N7)<br>[09 §5 Sequencing (post-1.0)](09-future-roadmap.md#5-sequencing-post-10)<br>[01 §6 Modularity & extension points](01-architecture.md#6-modularity--extension-points) ("Neural module" row)<br>[01 §3 Process & thread model](01-architecture.md#3-process--thread-model) (inference on worker threads, roadmap)<br>[01 §8 Security & privacy](01-architecture.md#8-security--privacy) (on-device only)<br>[02 §1 Stack at a glance](02-tech-stack.md#1-stack-at-a-glance) ("Neural (roadmap)" = ONNX Runtime)<br>[02 §6 Licensing summary (third-party components)](02-tech-stack.md#6-licensing-summary-third-party-components)<br>[05 §1 The Processor contract and AudioBlock](05-code-skeletons.md#1-the-processor-contract-and-audioblock) (note 9: `AsyncModelProcessor`)<br>[05 §4 ModuleSlot — click-free, latency-constant bypass](05-code-skeletons.md#4-moduleslot--click-free-latency-constant-bypass)<br>[04 §2 Annotated tree](04-project-structure.md#2-annotated-tree) (`core/include/flub/neural/`, `core/src/neural/`)<br>[04 §6.2 Adding a DSP module to the chain](04-project-structure.md#62-adding-a-dsp-module-to-the-chain)<br>[07 §2 Phase overview](07-roadmap.md#2-phase-overview) (Phase 4 post-1.0: neural features) | Neural framework (`flub_core`, namespace `flub`):<br>• `core/include/flub/neural/ModelRunner.h`: `ModelRunner` (`describe()` → `ModelDescription`: frame size, input channels as a mono downmix or planar, control count, `ControlKind` `BroadbandGain` / `ChannelGains`, expected sample rate; `prepare()`, `reset()` and `run()` off the audio thread)<br>• `core/include/flub/neural/FrameQueue.h`: wait-free SPSC queue of fixed-size float frames with a sequence header<br>• `core/include/flub/neural/AsyncModelProcessor.h`, `core/src/neural/AsyncModelProcessor.cpp`: a `Processor` with one polling worker thread; latency `frameSize × (1 + safetyFrames)`; the control from frame *k* applied to frame *k*; last good control held, then a ramp to neutral after `fallbackAfterFrames` misses or failures; controls clamped to `[0, maxGain]` and ramped over `controlRampMs`; stale frames skipped and late results dropped; `getDeadlineMisses()` / `getModelFailures()` / `getFramesProcessed()`; `process()` and `reset()` `FLUB_NONBLOCKING`<br>• `core/include/flub/neural/Eligibility.h`, `core/src/neural/Eligibility.cpp`: `isEligible (profile, modelLatencySamples, sampleRate, context)` in 10 ms reference frames, `neuralLatencyBudgetFrames()`<br>• `core/include/flub/neural/ReferenceRunners.h`, `core/src/neural/ReferenceRunners.cpp`: `IdentityRunner`, `ConstantGainRunner`, `FailingRunner`<br>• `core/include/flub/engine/ProcessingChain.h`, `core/src/engine/ProcessingChain.cpp`: the neural slot, after the gate and before the EQ and every dynamics stage; `setNeuralModel (runner, NeuralSlotConfig)` / `clearNeuralModel()` (non-RT, swapped in at the next `prepare()`, which `needsReprepare()` requests); `prepare()` applies `isEligible()` to the prepared latency profile and checks the model's sample rate, adds its latency only when it is in the chain and otherwise reports why (`getNeuralStatus()`, `NeuralSlotState`, `neuralSlotReason()`); `setNeuralBypass()` (latency-constant ModuleSlot fade); `getNeuralCounters()`; empty by default, then skipped like the gate outside Quality; `AsyncModelProcessor::releaseResources()` stops the worker of a model that is installed but out of the chain<br>Enabling architecture it builds on:<br>• `core/include/flub/dsp/Processor.h`: prepare / reset / process / latencySamples contract; `process` and `reset` are `FLUB_NONBLOCKING`<br>• `core/include/flub/common/Realtime.h`<br>• `core/include/flub/engine/ModuleSlot.h`, `core/src/engine/ModuleSlot.cpp`: dry path delayed by module latency, crossfaded bypass, pre-roll<br>• `core/include/flub/engine/ProcessingChain.h`, `core/src/engine/ProcessingChain.cpp`: sums slot latencies per latency profile; profile-gated modules (the STFT gate is in the chain only in Quality, the precedent for model eligibility)<br>• `core/include/flub/engine/MixEngine.h`, `core/src/engine/MixEngine.cpp`: pads strips to equal latency<br>• `core/include/flub/engine/MacroMap.h`, `core/src/engine/MacroMap.cpp`: table-driven control contributions a model's control outputs could feed<br>**Not present:** any ONNX Runtime dependency or real `ModelRunner`, model manifests, any model (N1–N7), a host that installs a model (the app, plug-in and CLI never call `setNeuralModel()`), parameters or a UI card for the slot, per-band / mask controls, and the neural status and counters in the UI.<br>Round 8 / hardening wave A (green on CI in run 16): `app/Source/engine/AudioEngineHost.*` `setNeuralModel` / `clearNeuralModel` (a factory makes a runner for every engine the host builds, installed through `MixEngine`'s `ChainSetup` before `prepare()`, so a model install or removal goes through the crossfaded swap, R1.5); `core/include/flub/neural/AsyncModelProcessor.h` and `ProcessingChain`: offline (waiting) mode and the `BlockTooLarge` status | `tests/test_neural.cpp`, all 14 cases (the real worker thread with scripted stand-in models):<br>• `Neural: latencySamples is frameSize * (1 + safetyFrames) and the identity model delays the input by exactly that`<br>• `Neural: the model receives consecutive input frames as a mono downmix or as planar channels`<br>• `Neural: the control computed from a frame is applied to exactly that frame's samples`<br>• `Neural: a constant -6 dB model scales the delayed input by -6 dB once the control ramp has settled`<br>• `Neural: channel-gain controls scale each output channel separately`<br>• `Neural: a blocked model counts each missed frame, holds the last good control, then crossfades to neutral after K misses`<br>• `Neural: a failing model counts failures, holds the last good control and falls back to neutral`<br>• `Neural: non-finite controls count as failures and controls are clamped to [0, maxGain]`<br>• `Neural: reset() clears the delay and the controls without stopping the worker`<br>• `Neural: process() and reset() are allocation-free`<br>• `Neural: an invalid runner is inert and a sample-rate mismatch keeps the latency without running the model`<br>• `Neural: eligibility follows the latency-profile rule`<br>• `Neural: repeated create, prepare, process and destroy joins the worker cleanly`<br>`tests/test_neural_slot.cpp`, all 10 cases (the chain's neural slot with stand-in models on the real worker thread; green on CI in run 14):<br>• `NeuralSlot: with no model the latency is the reference value per profile and the output bit-identical to an emptied slot's`<br>• `NeuralSlot: an identity model in Quality adds exactly its latency L and delays the chain's output by L: static settings / with the Boost macro`<br>• `NeuralSlot: a 2-frame model is ineligible in Low Latency: slot bypassed, latency unchanged, status says why`<br>• `NeuralSlot: a sample-rate mismatch, an invalid description or a failing prepare keep the slot out with the reason`<br>• `NeuralSlot: a constant -6 dB (or +12 dB) model before the limiter still holds the true-peak ceiling on hot material: full Music boost / the maximizer alone`<br>• `NeuralSlot: bypassing an active model fades to the latency-compensated dry path without changing the latency`<br>• `NeuralSlot: process() with an active model is allocation-free (bypass toggles, NaN reset and mode switch included)`<br>• `NeuralSlot: clearNeuralModel restores the original latency and output after the next prepare()`<br>`tests/test_rtsan.cpp` (RTSan build only): `hasNonblockingProcess<AsyncModelProcessor>` and `hasNonblockingReset<AsyncModelProcessor>` static_asserts, and `ProcessingChain::setNeuralBypass` / `getNeuralStatus` / `getNeuralCounters` as `FLUB_NONBLOCKING`; the Neural and NeuralSlot cases run under RealtimeSanitizer there.<br>Contracts a neural module relies on:<br>• `tests/test_engine.cpp`: `ModuleSlot: bypassed slot is a pure latency-compensated delay and toggling is click-free`; `Chain: latency per profile and constant under module bypass` (despite its name, it checks only per-profile ordering, the < 5 ms / < 2.5 ms bounds and the re-prepare flag; it does not toggle modules); `Chain: everything bypassed = input delayed by the chain latency (bit-transparent path)`; `MixEngine: strips are summed, padded to equal latency and master-limited`; `Chain: process() is allocation-free in every mode and profile`; `MacroMap: every mode has five named macros that engage modules`<br>• `tests/test_primitives.cpp`: `SpscRing: FIFO order, capacity and overflow drop`<br>• `tests/test_rtsan.cpp`: `RTSan: an allocation inside a nonblocking function stops the process (sanitizer self-test)`<br>The Neural cases are green on CI in run 12 (all four compilers, ASan + UBSan, RTSan).<br>Round 8 / hardening wave A (green on CI in run 16):<br>• `Neural: offline mode runs the model inside process(), so a render faster than real time gets every frame's result`<br>• `NeuralSlot: an Offline render faster than real time applies every frame's model result, reproducibly`<br>• `NeuralSlot: a host buffer longer than the model's safety frames keeps it out in real time (BlockTooLarge)`<br>• `App: neural model install and removal mid-stream go through the swap (model in the new engine, no click, no gap)` | Partially implemented | The neural framework of 09 §1.1 exists in the core and is tested on its real worker thread. Asserted:<br>• `latencySamples()` = `frameSize × (1 + safetyFrames)` for 0, 1, 2 and 5 safety frames; with one safety frame the identity model's output is the input delayed by exactly L, bit for bit, with odd host blocks (1 to 64 samples);<br>• the model sees consecutive frames as the mean of the channels (mono) or per channel (planar);<br>• a model returning 1 / level of its frame leaves every frame at unity within 1e-5 after the ramp, so each control lands on its own frame, not one early or late;<br>• a constant −6 dB model gives −6 dB within 0.01 dB (every sample within 1e-6 of the delayed input × gain); per-channel controls scale each channel;<br>• a model blocked on a gate: each missed frame is counted exactly (8 of 8), the last good control holds for K − 1 misses, then a 48-sample ramp to unity with no step larger than the ramp slope; after release the stale frames are skipped and the model's control returns;<br>• `run()` returning `false` and NaN controls count as failures (exactly 6, 7, 19 and 14 in the scenarios), with the same hold-then-neutral fallback; controls clamp to `[0, maxGain]`; the output stays finite;<br>• `reset()` with a frame in flight clears the delay line, keeps the worker running and calls the runner's `reset()` before the next frame;<br>• `process()` and `reset()` make 0 allocations, including while the model is blocked and the input queue overflows;<br>• a null runner or an invalid description is inert (latency 0, audio untouched); a sample-rate mismatch keeps the latency at unity gain without running the model;<br>• eligibility: Low Latency ≤ 1 reference frame (480 / 481 samples at 48 kHz, 441 / 442 at 44.1 kHz), Balanced ≤ 2, Quality and offline any; invalid latency or rate never;<br>• 50 create / prepare / re-prepare / process / destroy cycles join the worker cleanly; an explicit poll interval is capped at 100 ms.<br>Run 100 times in a row locally without a failure (the waits are on explicit conditions, bounded only as hang guards). The existing extension points still hold: any Processor declares a fixed `latencySamples()`, ModuleSlot keeps bypass latency-constant, `ProcessingChain::getLatencySamples()` sums every slot's latency (1352 / 192 / 100 smp, 28.2 / 4.0 / 2.1 ms at 48 kHz for Quality / Balanced / Low Latency, re-measured with the CLI; the test asserts Balanced < 5 ms and Low Latency < 2.5 ms).<br><br>The chain's neural slot (`tests/test_neural_slot.cpp`, 8 cases, green on CI in run 14; each passed 100 consecutive local runs):<br>• with no model, or a model installed and cleared, the latency is exactly 1352 / 192 / 100 samples (Quality / Balanced / Low Latency at 48 kHz) and the output bit-identical to a chain that never had one;<br>• an identity model with L = 512 in Quality adds exactly 512 samples (latency and the `latencyMs` meter) and the output is the model-free output delayed by L (within 1e-6 with static settings, 1e-2 with the Boost macro);<br>• a 960-sample model is ineligible in Low Latency (slot out, latency and output unchanged, the reason names the latency profile), active in Balanced after a re-prepare, out again in Low Latency, and active in any profile in the Offline context;<br>• a sample-rate mismatch, an invalid description and a runner whose `prepare()` throws each keep the slot out with their reason and a bit-identical output;<br>• a constant −6 dB or +12 dB model ahead of the maximizer keeps the 4× true peak within the ceiling + 0.15 dB and the sample peak at the ceiling, with no safety clip, with the full Music boost and with the maximizer alone (where its effect on the loudness is measurable);<br>• the slot's bypass fades to the latency-compensated dry path (within 1e-6 after the fade) without changing the latency;<br>• `process()` with an active model makes 0 allocations in all three profiles, including bypass toggles, a NaN reset and a mode switch;<br>• `clearNeuralModel()` restores the original latency and a bit-identical output after the next `prepare()`, and the counters restart.<br><br>**Why Partially implemented:** there is no inference runtime and no model. No ONNX Runtime `ModelRunner`, no model manifest and none of N1–N7 exist; the tests use stand-in runners. The chain now has a neural slot that applies `isEligible()` at `prepare()`, but no shipped code installs a model (the app host can now install one through the engine swap, `AudioEngineHost::setNeuralModel`, tested with a reference runner, but nothing in the app, plug-in or CLI loads a model), and the slot has no parameters or UI card. Controls are linear gains only (per-band gains and masks need a band-split renderer), and the slot's status and miss / failure counters (`getNeuralStatus()`, `getNeuralCounters()`) are not shown in the UI.<br><br>Hardening wave A (green on CI in run 16): an offline render (batch / export) runs the model inside `process()` so every frame gets its result however fast the render is, and a host buffer longer than the model's safety frames is reported as `BlockTooLarge` instead of counting structural deadline misses. |

the hardware side of the claim is not verified.<br><br>Tested:<br>• all 8 profiles are matched from 12 typical endpoint names (including PDP Airlite, Victrix and LVL50 for the PDP brand), with no false positives on Razer Blade Stealth, Stealthy Mic, Reconnect Audio, Airliner Lounge Speakers, "Headphones (Victrixx)" and 4 other non-Turtle-Beach names;<br>• ceiling caps are asserted at −1 dBTP for a USB Stealth and for a generic analog (Realtek) output, −2 for Bluetooth A2DP (via the platform hint) and −3 for hands-free; 2.4 GHz dongles are classified as USB by `detectConnection` and are not asserted separately;<br>• hands-free is detected from names and from narrowband formats (≤ 16 kHz with ≤ 1 output channel; the test covers 16 kHz mono);<br>• the advice warns about Superhuman Hearing, virtual surround and Game / Chat routing;<br>• the embedded database matches the JSON: profile count and id, matchAny, exclude, notes, specificity and gamingPreset are compared; musicPreset, ceiling, connection and labVerified are not;<br>• the chain runs at 11 rates from 8 to 192 kHz, in both modes, with Boost and every macro at 100 %, in the default Balanced profile only. The sample peak of both output channels stays at or below −1 dBFS. Latency is under 6 ms at 22.05 kHz and above and under 12 ms at 8 / 16 kHz (Balanced only; Quality is 30.2 ms at 44.1 kHz and 144 ms at 8 kHz).<br><br>Measured with the CLI and re-measured (Balanced / Low Latency): 8 kHz 92 / 60 smp = 11.5 / 7.5 ms (only 0.5 ms under the 12 ms bound), 16 kHz 7.0 / 4.25 ms, 44.1 kHz 4.13 / 2.18 ms, 48 kHz 4.00 / 2.08 ms, 96 kHz 3.25 / 1.54 ms. 03 §1.4: the Bluetooth preset (−2 dBTP ceiling) reads −2.05 dBTP at worst.<br><br>**Why Partial:**<br>1. Every profile is `labVerified:false`. Neither the device-lab plan in 10 §5 (2 h soak, loopback true peak ≤ cap, hot-plug and sleep ≤ 2 s, A2DP ↔ HFP switch, added latency ≤ 10 / 12 ms) nor roadmap item 1.10 has been run on a real headset, so compatibility rests on standard device classes, not measurement.<br>2. Linux `queryOutputTransport` returns Unknown, so Linux relies on name and format heuristics. The Windows and macOS transport queries are build-only and have never run against a device.<br>3. Applying the cap on a device change is now tested headlessly (`flub_app_tests`, green on CI in run 11): `EngineController::simulateOutputDevice`, the path a real device change takes (`updateDeviceProfile` → `applyDeviceProfile` → `AudioEngineHost::setMasterCeilingDb`), for a Bluetooth Stealth, a hands-free Stealth (16 kHz mono), a wired Recon, the hands-free one again and a Realtek output sets the connection, the advice and the master ceiling to −2 / −3 / −1 / −3 / −1 dBTP; two full-scale strips pushed about 11 dB over full scale then leave the output sample peak between cap − 0.6 dB and cap + 0.05 dB, with at least 6 dB of master gain reduction. The advice banner, switching back to the preferred output with a 5 s rescan and per-device dismissal have no automated test beyond the screenshot. The master limiter at the −2 / −3 dBTP caps is now tested directly, with the cap taken from `adviceFor()` for a Bluetooth Stealth endpoint (44.1 / 48 kHz) and a hands-free one (8 / 16 kHz) and set with `MixEngine::setMasterCeilingDb` as the app does: on material whose true peak is 9–10 dB over the cap, true peak ≤ cap + 0.1 dB on the 4× meter and an independent 4× interpolator, sample peak ≤ cap, no safety clamp (measured 0.045–0.050 dB below the cap). The air-exciter cut-off is tested too: at 8, 16 and 32 kHz, air at 100 % (also via the Clarity macro) leaves the output equal to the delayed input within 1e−6, while at 44.1 and 48 kHz it adds its harmonics and shelf. What remains untested on the app side is the banner, the preferred-output rescan and per-device dismissal.<br>4. The Game / Chat virtual-endpoint routing in 10 §4 needs the Windows driver / macOS HAL plug-in, which are designed only.<br>5. The headset microphone is not processed, and console-only use is out of scope by design.<br><br>docs/11 E17 (green on CI in run 16): hands-free (HFP) detection now covers rates up to 32 kHz (super-wideband), a name with "wireless" no longer implies USB, and Bluetooth evidence beats a profile's typical connection.<br><br>Wave B2 (local runs only, green on CI in run 17): the user's `device-profiles.json` override is read through `juce::File`, so a user-data folder with a non-ASCII name works on Windows whatever the ANSI code page, and a damaged override falls back to the shipped database.<br><br>Phase 3 batch 3 (local only): on a corpus of 130 Windows, macOS and PipeWire endpoint names (`tests/data/endpoint-names-corpus.txt`) 69 / 69 headset strings are in the right family and none of the 61 others match (63 / 69 and 16 false positives before), the owner's Windows 11 Turtle Beach names included; the headset enhancement answer is stored per output endpoint (found again after a Windows "2- " re-plug, a rename and without ids) and caps every strip. Not verified: the owner's Windows run, hardware ids, the device lab.<br><br>Phase 3 batch 4 (local only; not run on Windows): a fallback to speakers caps the bass lift at +3 dB (+17.3 -> +2.74 dB at 40 Hz re 1 kHz) instead of bypassing the bass engine; a correction saved on *Headset Earphone (Stealth 700 Gen 2)* applies after a re-plug as *(2- Stealth 700 Gen 2)*, and name-keyed files load and gain the ids; *Follow the system default output* persists and is honoured (fakes only).<br><br>docs/11 Phase 3 batch 5 (local, not yet on CI): Settings › Hearing takes the listener's headset sensitivity (no shipped profile has one, so without it the page says Unknown and nothing is applied), a listening-level cap and a daily dose kept per day; the per-ear profile editor reaches every strip's chain (right +6 dB -> R - L +6.00 dB). A +12 dB one-ear HF profile turns both ears down about 9 dB (owner decision). |

## Known gaps

Every row that is not **Implemented and tested**, with what is missing. Details and figures are in each row's Notes.

- **R1.1 Latency (Partially implemented).** The 10–12 ms end-to-end target is not measured. The ≈9.5 ms and ≈12–13 ms figures are estimates for the Windows virtual-driver path, which is design only; no loopback measurement has been made on a real device (07 item 1.2, 10 §5 test 8; the probe itself, `flub::latency::LatencyProbe` and `flubsound-cli latency-probe`, exists and is tested offline since docs/11 E42d, Phase 3 batch 1, green in CI run 36521915726), and the UI readout is reported, not measured. Today's Windows process-loopback path alone adds a 612-frame (12.75 ms) FIFO target, and the default Linux PipeWire quantum is ≈21 ms unless forced to 256 (01 §5.3). The docs now share one budget with explicit scopes (01 §5). Wave B2 (green on CI in run 17): the readout is now per strip, labelled an estimate and "--" without an audible path, and a strip is padded only within its sync group (docs/11 E42a / E40 part 3); the PipeWire quantum is added only when set (`setGraphQuantumMs`), nothing detects it yet.
- **R1.2 Audio backends (Partially implemented).** No test opens a device. CI run 36247109446 built the app and plug-in on Windows (WASAPI), macOS (CoreAudio) and Linux (ALSA / JACK); ASIO has no recorded build (it needs the Steinberg SDK). The native PipeWire `pw_filter` node, per-stream `node.latency`, the exclusive-mode policy (08 D2) and the ASIO single-client note (08 D10) are roadmap, and no device-lab results exist. The native PipeWire node (docs/11 E48, Phase 3 batch 2) is built and tested against a headless PipeWire locally but not offered in the app, and CI has no PipeWire server. Since docs/11 Phase 3 batch 5 (local, not on CI) the app offers the native PipeWire node as a device type and opens it on a first start with a PipeWire server; it is tested against a headless server only (the CI `pipewire` job, non-blocking, has not run), not on the distribution matrix.
- **R1.5 Buffer management (Partially implemented).** Glitch-free operation on the real audio thread is not exercised: the app's callback code is not RTSan-annotated (it is allocation-, free- and, on Linux, lock-tested with a fake device in `flub_app_tests`, green on CI in run 11), and `DriftCompensatedFifo` is proven in simulation only. The CPU-overload watchdog and the FIFO `Stats` in the UI exist and are tested with chosen samples and fake devices (green on CI in run 12); the opt-in automatic response to an overload (one latency-profile step down, rate-limited, never back up; `tests/app/test_app_overload_response.cpp`, green on CI in run 14) is tested the same way. Neither has seen a real overload. The crossfaded engine swap on structural changes now exists (round 8, green on CI in run 16) and is tested with a fake device, including allocation / free / lock probes over 100 swaps, but not on a real device. Missing: a real-device soak (07 items 1.1, 1.10; the glitch detector and an offline soak with parameter automation exist since docs/11 Phase 2 batch 2, green in CI run 36391842718) and driver clock slaving (design only); RealtimeKit on Linux exists since docs/11 E44 Phase A (Phase 3 batch 1, green in CI run 36521915726) but is tested against a mock rtkit on a private bus only, and no real-device run has checked the thread's policy (`chrt -p`) or an xrun count. Wave B2 (green on CI in run 17): the docs/11 E51 loopback guard, the capture restart at a new device rate and the stalled-callback quarantine are tested with fake devices only; the device banner with Retry / Choose output / Sound settings is built since docs/11 Phase 2 batch 2 (fake devices; green in CI run 36391842718), and the device callback's duration / interval histograms (E45) are recorded but not shown. Phase 3 batch 2's device recovery (E51: hot-plug, sleep / resume, exclusive-mode retries) is tested with fakes only; on Windows its `IMMNotificationClient` and power-notification code has never run. Phase 3 batch 3 (local only, not yet on CI) adds docs/11 E45's idle freeze of fed-silence strips and the p99.9 callback in the watchdog, tested offline and with fake timings; neither has run on a real device, and the shed ladder, battery mode, `flub-bench` and the CI perf gate are not built. Phase 3 batch 4 (local): the offline soak of the chain still finds clicks around −43 … −50 dB in its user rows (10 in 2 minutes of each row here, 13 on the batch 3 commit, so not introduced by batch 4; not triaged), so the nightly `soak` step fails without failing the run.
- **R3.5 Anti-clipping + THD protection (Partially implemented).** The governor now acts on measured THD+N of the saturator and the soft clipper over 25 ms windows (the clipper's share floored at the former clip-energy proxy over the same window, so it does not act later than before), tested as a unit and end to end (`tests/test_distortion.cpp`, green on CI in run 14). What remains is coverage: at the default protection strength (Off) it takes back only governed macro contributions, so distortion from base `max.drive` / `sat.drive` is measured but not limited (strength Normal / Strict also scales the base drives since docs/11 E06's slice, Phase 2 batch 1, green in CI run 36367474107, and since Phase 2 batch 2 (green in CI run 36391842718) it is chosen in the app, Settings › Processing or the Boost panel's governor chip; all Music macros 100 on a 50 Hz sine measured 6.0 % THD+N at Strict against the ≤ 3 % target until docs/11 E06 Phase 3, Phase 3 batch 1, green in CI run 36521915726: now 1.5 % at Normal and 1.6 % at Strict, Off still 20.0 %); the limiter's own gain-modulation distortion is a governor input only at Normal / Strict (docs/11 E06 step 1, Phase 2 batch 2, green in CI run 36391842718); at Normal / Strict the governor now runs a measured loop on the loudness-weighted, masking-limited residual of two spans with a harmonics policy for the bass harmonics generator (Phase 3 batch 1, green in CI run 36521915726), with provisional budgets awaiting a listening panel, a 4.3 dB transient dip while it backs off and no readout of its new readings yet; the bass harmonics generator and the air exciter are measured but deliberately not a governor input (their reading is now published in the meter bus, not shown); and the measured distortion is shown only as the governor chip's ~3 s average (tooltip) and the LoudnessPanel's Distortion / Harmonics bars. Phase 3 batch 2: E06's 3-minute row is not met on mastered music (LRA 3.95 LU at Normal; the bass engine's non-harmonic residual against the budget), and the budgets still need the E60 listening panel.
- **R4.1 Dark GUI (verified by build / manual run only).** No tests of the look (`flub_app_tests` checks the meter views' values, R4.2, and the header / Settings CPU and capture text, R1.5; since docs/11 E39, Phase 3 batch 1, green in CI run 36521915726, a layout test also asserts that in the Simple and Advanced views at 1100 × 700 .. 2560 × 1440 no control leaves the window and the Simple view's controls do not overlap); screenshots are uploaded, not diffed; Windows and macOS rendering are unverified (CI builds the app there but renders nothing). The UI scale setting and the high-contrast theme (roadmap 3.6) now exist, with their contrast ratios, theme switch, settings and minimum window sizes tested in `flub_app_tests` (green on CI in run 16), but not their rendering. Screen-reader live values, onboarding (1.6 / 3.6), localisation (3.7) and the dark plug-in editor (2.9) are missing.
- **R4.4 Tray + global hotkeys (Partially implemented).** Linux global hotkeys are implemented for X11 (`XGrabKey`) and tested under Xvfb (locally, and by the CI `sanitizers` job's Xvfb step, first green in run [36269443100](https://github.com/flubydooby-creator/Flubsound/actions/runs/36269443100) on commit `df28c48`, where the X11 case ran rather than skipped). Wayland sessions now use the xdg-desktop-portal GlobalShortcuts interface, tested only against a mock portal on a private `dbus-daemon` (green on CI in run 11, never on a real desktop); shortcuts the desktop declines, leaves unbound or binds to another key are now shown per action on the Hotkeys page (tested against the mock portal and with a fake service in `flub_app_tests`, green on CI in run 16). Start with the OS now exists on all three OSes, but only the Linux XDG autostart entry is tested; the Windows `Run`-key code has only been compiled (MinGW) and the macOS `SMAppService` code has been neither built against a real SDK nor run. The tray on every OS and Windows / macOS hotkeys are build-verified only with no recorded manual verification. Wave B2 (green on CI in run 17): the docs/11 E56 hotkey target and the Focus, Night, ChatMix and strip Bypass actions are tested headlessly with a fake hotkey service; there is no OSD, and the Wayland portal fixes (non-blocking probe, bus loss, stale answers, GNOME and localised trigger descriptions) are tested against the mock portal only. Batch 5 (local, not on CI): the on-screen display's Windows styles and exclusive-fullscreen check and the `ctl` socket's Winsock path are compiled by MinGW-w64 only; the in-game PresentMon check is gated on the owner's Windows PC; no Settings row for the OSD options yet.
- **R4.5 Per-app profiles and routing (Partially implemented).** macOS is unsupported (process taps, roadmap 3.2). Windows programmatic endpoint moves exist only in opt-in `FLUB_ENABLE_UNDOCUMENTED_ROUTING` builds, are not validated per Windows build (07 item 2.4) and need the unbuilt virtual driver. RoutingPanel (except its red *No apps are being processed* state, wave B2) and all Windows routing / capture code are untested (`AppRouting` itself is tested with a fake router and fake captures, green on CI in run 16); Linux moves are not run in CI. Automatic profile switching by foreground application (roadmap 2.5) now exists (round 8, green on CI in run 16): the switching logic, the controller, the settings and the panel are tested with a scripted foreground source and the Linux X11 source under Xvfb; the Windows and macOS sources are only syntax-checked (MinGW) or read, Wayland reports it unsupported, and no rule has been tried with a real game. Wave B2 (docs/11 E47a, green on CI in run 17): *Automatic* no longer picks endpoint moves that a default Windows build cannot make, and process capture is gated at build 19041 instead of 20348; that gate has not run on a 19041–19045 machine. Phase 3 batch 2's doubling guard, route journal and Tournament mode (docs/11 E47 / E55) are tested with fakes; the Windows code has not run on Windows, and the Linux half of the journal (WirePlumber's restore-stream entries) is open. Tournament mode (docs/11 E55's app wiring, Phase 3 batch 3, local only) switches itself on while a known anti-cheat service runs and freezes routing, automatic profiles and the foreground poll, tested with a fake service list; the Windows service query and the 1 h ETW trace against real anti-cheat titles have not run. The docs/11 E22 chat duck and ChatMix (Phase 3 batch 4, local) are tested with synthetic formant speech, fake devices and tones only; a loud teammate at −14 LUFS still loses 0.82 dB short-term under explosions (the sub-limiter is not built), and nothing has been played against real Discord captures or Sonar ChatMix on Windows. Batch 5 (local, not on CI): Linux's route journal clears WirePlumber restore-stream entries (WirePlumber 0.4 tested, 0.5 not); while an app with a `media.role` sits on a strip, WirePlumber 0.4 also starts every other app of that role there (found, not fixed).
- **R4.6 Virtual device / cable (Partially implemented).** No virtual driver exists: the Windows WaveRT driver (only its ABI header exists) and the macOS Audio Server Plug-in are designed only (roadmap 2.1–2.3, 3.1). The native PipeWire node exists since docs/11 E48 (Phase 3 batch 2, green in CI run 36575196651: its own sinks, registry linking, `node.latency`, a "PipeWire" device type), tested against a headless `pipewire` + `wireplumber`, but the app does not offer it yet and it has not run on a desktop session. On Linux, the router now links the sink monitors to the device inputs with `pw-link` (docs/11 E48a, Phase 2 batch 1, green in CI run 36367474107), tested against recorded `pw-dump` output but not on a real PipeWire system; multi-strip mapping has no UI. The Linux sinks, PipeWire drop-ins and cable detection are untested.
- **R5.3 Neural readiness (Partially implemented).** The framework exists and is tested (`core/include/flub/neural/`: `ModelRunner`, `AsyncModelProcessor` with a fixed latency, frame-exact controls, counted deadline misses and failures with hold-then-neutral fallback, and the `isEligible()` profile rule; 13 `Neural:` cases, green on CI in run 12), but there is no ONNX Runtime, no model manifest and no model. `ProcessingChain` now has a neural slot that applies the eligibility rule at `prepare()` (8 `NeuralSlot:` cases, green on CI in run 14), and the app host can now install a model through the crossfaded engine swap (tested with a reference runner, green on CI in run 16), but nothing ships or loads a model and the slot has no parameters or UI; controls are broadband / per-channel gains only, and the neural status and counters are not in the UI.
- **R6.1 Turtle Beach headsets (Partially implemented).** Every device profile is `labVerified:false`; the device-lab plan (10 §5, 07 item 1.10) has not been run on a real headset. The Linux transport query returns Unknown; the app applying the cap on a device change is now tested headlessly (`flub_app_tests`, green on CI in run 11), but the banner and preferred-output behaviour are untested; the master limiter at −2 / −3 dBTP and the air-exciter cut-off are now tested directly (green on CI); Game / Chat virtual-endpoint routing needs the unbuilt drivers; the headset microphone is not processed. Phase 3 batch 3 (local only): the endpoint-name matching now needs a vendor or headset word beside ordinary words (130-name corpus, 0 false positives) and the per-endpoint "Headset enhancement is ON" cap is tested headlessly; the owner's Windows 11 run with his Turtle Beach headset, hardware ids (USB VID:PID, IOKit, PipeWire ids) and lab-verified entries remain. Phase 3 batch 4 (local): the correction keyed by the endpoint's identity, the preferred output with its identity, *Follow the system default output* and the safe speaker profile's +3 dB bass cap are tested with fake backends only; the dongle re-plug on the owner's Windows 11 PC and the device lab's power cycles remain; no shipped profile carries a sensitivity, so the docs/11 E32 (c) listening-level estimate is off for every headset. Batch 5 (local, not on CI): Settings › Hearing takes the listener's sensitivity; no shipped profile has one, so the hearing guard stays off by default, and the Windows / macOS volume reads for it have not run on real systems.

Rows marked Implemented and tested can still name untested sub-parts in their Notes: notably R2.8 (the Music mode bands are not asserted), R2.9 (Gaming Boost staging and real-game efficacy), R2.10 (the ModuleSlot test does not prove a smooth crossfade between differing paths; match accuracy on hot programme, where the ceiling is involved, is now asserted within 0.5 LU by the docs/11 E37 case, green on CI in run 16), R3.1 (the true-peak bound is measured, not proven), R3.2 (AutoLevel convergence is now asserted after a large level drop, green on CI in run 16), R4.2 (painting and layout, the IEC deflection scale, peak hold and the `LoudnessPanel` momentary / integrated / LRA / true-peak readouts; its `flub_app_tests` cases are green on CI in run 11), R5.1 (progress output and command-line parsing into `batch`; the in-app Export / batch dialog's painting, and its tests are green on CI in run 16) and R5.2 (no live recording of the app's output; WAV, and FLAC in the app, always stereo; the static ceiling trim and the DAW-bounce route are untested). Closed in this pass, by tests green on CI in run 9: the LoudnessMatch unit tests (R2.1), the Width and Clarity macros (R2.8), A/B and bypass click-freedom (R2.10) and the CLI loudness-target loop (R3.2, R5.1). The documentation inconsistencies an earlier pass recorded are fixed: the Windows driver README §6 master-limiter count and its ≤ 15 ms goal (it now uses the single budget of 01 §5), the missing R4.6 row in 06 §14, the 03 §0.1 double-precision list, the stale 08 D1 note on drift tests, and the stale comment in the CD-band limiter test.
